and
\zeta_{i}\equiv 0
on
Q_{T}\setminus\mathcal{Q}_{i}
.
Then there is
C>0
such that

|(\zeta_{i})_{t}|\leq\frac{C}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}=\frac{C2^{i+1}}{\theta T}\quad\text{ and }\quad|\nabla\zeta_{i}|\leq\frac{C}{\rho_{i}-\rho_{i+1}}=\frac{C2^{i+1}}{4\rho}\quad\text{for all }i\geq 0.

(3.76)

Define

F_{i}=\|{\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\zeta_{i}}\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(A_{i+1,i})}
.
Applying (
3.74
) with
k=k_{i+1}
and
\zeta=\zeta_{i}
gives

F_{i}\leq C\lambda\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}|^{2}\Big(\zeta_{i}|(\zeta_{i})_{t}|+|\nabla\zeta_{i}|^{2}\Big)dxdt+T\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|A_{i+1,i}|\Big\}^{1/2}.

(3.77)

Using derivative estimates (
3.76
) for
\zeta
, we obtain

\displaystyle F_{i}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Big(\big[2^{i}(\theta T)^{-1/2}+2^{i}\big]\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i})}+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2}\Big)

(3.78)

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda 2^{i}(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2}\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}+C\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2}.

Then, it follows from Hölder’s inequality, (
3.77
)
and (
3.78
) that

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(A_{i+1,i+1})}|A_{i+1,i+1}|^{1/2-1/{\mu_{5}}}

(3.79)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\zeta_{i}\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(A_{i+1,i+1})}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2-1/{\mu_{5}}}\leq CF_{i}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2-1/{\mu_{5}}}.

Note that

\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}\geq\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i})}\geq(k_{i+1}-k_{i})|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2}
.
Thus,

|A_{i+1,i}|\leq(k_{i+1}-k_{i})^{-2}\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{2}\leq C4^{i}M_{0}^{-2}\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{2}.

(3.80)

Then it follows (
3.79
), (
3.78
) and (
3.80
) that

\displaystyle\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Big\{2^{i}(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2}\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{i}M_{0}^{-1}\|q^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot 2^{i-\frac{2i}{\mu_{5}}}M_{0}^{-1+2/{\mu_{5}}}\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{1-2/{\mu_{5}}}

\displaystyle\leq C4^{i}\lambda\Big\{(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})M_{0}^{-1+2/{\mu_{5}}}+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}M_{0}^{-2+2/{\mu_{5}}}\Big\}\|{\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{2-2/{\mu_{5}}}.

Let
Y_{i}=\|\bar{q}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}
,
B=4
and

\displaystyle D_{1}

\displaystyle=C\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2}M_{0}^{-1+2/{\mu_{5}}}=C\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2}M_{0}^{-\mu_{6}},

\displaystyle D_{2}

\displaystyle=C\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}M_{0}^{-2+2/{\mu_{5}}}=C\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}M_{0}^{-1-\mu_{6}}.

We obtain

Y_{i+1}\leq B^{i}(D_{1}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{6}}+D_{1}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{6}})
for all
i\geq 0
.
We now determine
M_{0}
so that

Y_{0}\leq(2D_{1})^{-1/\mu_{6}}B^{-1/{\mu_{6}^{2}}},\quad Y_{0}\leq(2D_{2})^{-1/\mu_{6}}B^{-1/{\mu_{6}^{2}}}.

(3.81)

This condition is met if

M_{0}\geq C\big[\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-1/2})\big]^{1/\mu_{6}}Y_{0},\quad M_{0}\geq C\big[\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}Y_{0}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}.

Since
Y_{0}\leq\|\bar{q}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}
, it suffices to choose
M_{0}
as

M_{0}=C\Big\{\big[\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-1/2})\big]^{1/\mu_{6}}+\big[\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}\Big(\|\bar{q}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}+\|\bar{q}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\Big).

(3.82)

Then by condition (
3.81
), applying (
A.5
) in Lemma
A.2
for
m=2
gives

\displaystyle{\lim_{i\to\infty}}Y_{i}=0
. (Alternatively, Lemma
A.1
can be used in this case.)
Hence,

\int_{\theta T}^{T}\int_{B(x_{0},\rho/4)}|\bar{q}^{(M_{0})}|^{2}dxdt=0.

(3.83)

Since
\bar{q}(x,t)\in C(U\times(0,\infty)
, it follows from (
3.83
) that
\bar{q}(x,t)\leq M_{0}
in
B(x_{0},\rho/4)\times(\theta T,T)
.
Replace
q
by
-q
and
\psi
by
-\psi
and
use the same argument we obtain
|\bar{q}(x,t)|\leq M_{0}
in
B(x_{0},\rho/4)\times(\theta T,T)
.
Now by covering
U^{\prime}
by finitely many such balls
B(x_{0},\rho/4)
, we come to conclusion

|\bar{q}(x,t)|\leq M_{0}\quad\text{in }U^{\prime}\times(\theta T,T).

(3.84)

By the choice of
M_{0}
, we obtain (
3.70
) from (
3.84
).

In the above proof of (
3.70
), we can work with
q
instead of
\bar{q}
, with

\frac{\partial q}{\partial t}=\nabla\cdot\big(K(|\nabla p|)\nabla p\big)_{t},

(3.85)

instead of equation (
3.72
), then the term
\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}
can be removed and we obtain the desired estimate (
3.71
) for
p_{t}
.
∎

Remark 3.14
.

The main difference between estimate of
\bar{p}_{t}
in (
3.70
) and the estimate of
p_{t}
in (
3.71
) is the involvement of
\psi_{t}
.
Though we cannot derive (
3.71
) from (
3.70
), in the following development we will focus on
\bar{p}_{t}
only.

The following estimates can be easily derived (by the mean of Young’s inequality) from corresponding ones in
[
18
]
. They will be used in finding
L^{\infty}
-estimates for
\bar{p}_{t}
in terms of initial data and boundary data.
We use the following notation:

\displaystyle m_{1}(t)=1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,

\displaystyle m_{2}(t)=1+A^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,

(3.86)

\displaystyle m_{3}(t)=1+\beta^{\frac{1}{1-a}}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}f^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}=A+A^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau.

(3.87)

Theorem 3.15

(cf.
[
18
]
Theorems 4.4 and 4.5)
.

(i)
One has for all
t\geq 0
that

J_{H}[p](t)+\|{\bar{p}_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,t))}^{2}\leq C\Big(\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+J_{H}[p](0)+(t+1)\sup_{[0,t]}f+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big),

(3.88)

and for all
t\geq 1
that

J_{H}[p](t)+\|{\bar{p}_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(t-1/2,t))}^{2}\leq C\Big(\|{\bar{p}(t-1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}f+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big).

(3.89)

Now, assume that the Degree Condition holds.

(ii)
For
0<t_{0}<1
and
t\geq t_{0}
, one has

\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\,t_{0}^{-1}L_{5}(t_{0})+C\int_{0}^{t}f(\tau)+\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,

(3.90)

where
L_{5}(t_{0})=L_{5}(t_{0};[p_{0},\psi])\mathbin{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathbin{=\kern-2.0pt=}}\limits^{\rm def}}}1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+M_{f}(t_{0})^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau
.

For all
t\geq 1
, one has

J_{H}[p](t),\ \|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq Cm_{1}(t)\quad\text{for all }t\geq 1.

(3.91)

(iii)
If
A<\infty
then there is
T>1
such that

J_{H}[p](t),\ \|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq Cm_{2}(t)\quad\text{for all }t>T,

(3.92)

\limsup_{t\to\infty}J_{H}[p](t),\ \limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{A}_{1}.

(3.93)

(iv)
If
\beta<\infty
then there is
T>1
such that

J_{H}[p](t),\ \|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq Cm_{3}(t)\quad\text{for all }t>T.

(3.94)

We now state our main estimates of
\bar{p}_{t}
.
In addition to
\mathcal{K}_{1,t}
defined by (
3.53
), we will also use

\mathcal{K}_{2,t}=1+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau.

(3.95)

Theorem 3.16
.

Assume (
SDC
).
For
t>0
, one has

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(1+t)\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,t}\Big\}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{2}}

(3.96)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{1+t^{-\frac{1}{2\mu_{6}}}+t^{\frac{1}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}.

For
t\geq 3/2
, one has

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big).

(3.97)

Above, the positive constant
C
depends on
U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Applying (
3.70
) with
T_{0}=0
,
T=t
and
\theta=1/2
, an then applying Young’s inequality to the term

\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,t))}^{\mu_{6}/(\mu_{6}+1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\sup_{[0,t]}\|\nabla p\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a})^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\Big[1+\sqrt{2/t\,}\Big]^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}+\big[t^{1/2}\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\Big(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,t))}\Big).

Using (
3.88
) to estimate
\sup_{[0,t]}\|\nabla p\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}
and
\|{\bar{p}_{t}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,t))}
in the previous inequality, we find that

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big\{1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(1+t)\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,t}\Big\}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big\{1+t^{-\frac{1}{2\mu_{6}}}+t^{\frac{1}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}\Big(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(1+t)\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,t}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then inequality (
3.96
) follows.

Now, consider
t\geq 3/2
.
Applying estimate (
3.70
) to the interval
[t-1/2,t]
, i.e.,
T_{0}=t-1/2
and
T=1/2
, with
\theta=1/2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|{\nabla p}\|_{L^{2-a}(U)}^{2-a}\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(t-1/2,t))}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}+\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(t-1/2,t))}\Big).

Let
m(t)=\sup_{[t-3/2,t]}f+\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau
.
Utilizing estimate (
3.89
), we have

\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big(1+\sup_{\tau\in[t-3/2,t-1]}\|{\bar{p}(\tau)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+m(t)\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\\
\cdot\Big\{\Big(\|{\bar{p}(t-1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+m(t)\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}+\Big(\|{\bar{p}(t-1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+m(t)\Big)\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.98)

By Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\bar{p}_{t}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{1+\sup_{\tau\in[t-3/2,t-1]}\|{\bar{p}(\tau)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+m(t)\Big\}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}.

Then inequality (
3.97
) is obtained by using (
3.29
) to estimate
\|\bar{p}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}
for
\tau\leq t-1
.
∎

Let

\mathcal{A}_{2}=\limsup_{t\to\infty}m_{2}(t)=1+A^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,

(3.99)

\mathcal{A}_{3}=1+A^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}.

(3.100)

We have from (
3.87
), (
3.99
) and (
3.100
) the relation:

\mathcal{A}_{1}\leq C\mathcal{A}_{2}\leq C^{\prime}\mathcal{A}_{3}.

(3.101)

Theorem 3.17
.

Assume (
SDC
).

(i)
If
A<\infty
then

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}+\mathcal{A}_{1}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}\big)^{1/2}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big).

(3.102)

Consequently, denoting
\mu_{8}=\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-a}{(2-a)(\mu_{6}+1)}
, one has

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\mathcal{A}_{3}^{\mu_{8}}.

(3.103)

(ii)
If
\beta<\infty
, then there is
T>0
such that for all
t>T
,

\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big(1+\beta^{\frac{1}{1-a}}+\sup_{\tau\in[t-3/2,t]}f(\tau)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big).

(3.104)

Above, the positive constant
C
depends on
U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(i) We follow the proof in Theorem
3.16
.
Observe that

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\leq\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-2}^{t-1}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\leq 2\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau.

(3.105)

The limit estimates (
3.30
) and (
3.105
) yield

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big(\sup_{[t-3/2,t-1]}\|\bar{p}\|_{L^{2}}+m(t)\Big)\leq C\mathcal{A}_{1}.

Combining this with (
3.98
), we obtain

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\big(1+\mathcal{A}_{1}\big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\big[\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\big]\Big)\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}+\mathcal{A}_{1}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then (
3.102
) follows this and relation (
3.101
).
Elementary calculations will give (
3.103
) from (
3.102
).

(ii) We use (
3.98
) and Young’s inequality again, this time, with estimate (
3.31
); it results in (
3.104
).
∎

While (
3.102
) gives an asymptotic estimate for
\bar{p}_{t}
in the case
A<\infty
, the next result covers the case
A=\infty
.

Theorem 3.18
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Let
L_{6}=\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
in general case, and
L_{6}=\beta^{\frac{1}{1-a}}
in case
\beta<\infty
, and define

N(t)=1+L_{6}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau,\quad h(t)=N(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big).

(3.106)

If

\lim_{t\to\infty}h(t)^{\frac{2(\mu_{6}+1)}{\mu_{6}}}e^{-d_{1}\int_{2}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h^{\prime}(t)}{h(t)}N^{b}(t)=0,

(3.107)

where
b
is defined in (
2.9
) and
d_{1}>0
appears in (
3.111
) below,
then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{h(t)\Big(N(t)^{b}\|{\psi_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\Big[N(t)^{b}\|{\psi_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big]^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\Big\},

(3.108)

where
C>0
depends on
U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Applying (
3.70
) to the interval
[t-1,t]
with
\theta=\frac{1}{2}
, we have

\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big[\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1,t]}J_{H}[p](\tau)\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\Big]\\
\cdot\Big(\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{2}(U)}\Big).

(3.109)

In the following
T>2
is sufficiently large. By (
3.91
) in the general case, and by (
3.94
) in case
\beta<\infty
, we have

J_{H}[p](\tau)\leq C\Big(1+L_{6}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big),\quad\tau>T.

Therefore,

\sup_{[t-1,t]}J_{H}[p](\tau)\leq CN(t).

(3.110)

Recall inequality (5.13) in
[
18
]
: there is
d_{1}>0
such that for
t>
we have

\frac{d}{dt}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|^{2}\leq-d_{1}N(t)^{-b}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|^{2}+C\|{\psi_{t}(t)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(t)^{b}.

(3.111)

Therefore, for
t^{\prime}\in(T,\infty)

\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t^{\prime})}\|^{2}\leq e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t^{\prime}}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+\int_{T}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t^{\prime}}N(s)^{-b}ds}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau.

Then taking supremum in
t^{\prime}
over the interval
[t-1,t]
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{p}_{t}}\|^{2}\leq e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t-1}N^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t-1}N^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\int_{T}^{t}e^{d_{1}\int_{T}^{\tau}N^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau.

Thanks to the fact
N(t)\geq 1
, we have

e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t-1}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}=e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}e^{d_{1}\int_{t-1}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\leq e^{d_{1}}e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}.

Hence,

\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{p}_{t}}\|^{2}\\
\leq C\Big(e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\int_{T}^{t}e^{d_{1}\int_{T}^{\tau}N^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big)\\
=C\Big(e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}N^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big).

(3.112)

By (
3.109
), (
3.110
) and (
3.112
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq Ch(t)\Big\{\Big(e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}N(s)^{-b}ds}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}

\displaystyle+\Big(e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}N(s)^{-b}ds}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Big(h(t)^{\frac{2(\mu_{6}+1)}{\mu_{6}}}e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+h(t)^{\frac{2(\mu_{6}+1)}{\mu_{6}}}\int_{T}^{t}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}N(s)^{-b}ds}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big)^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big(h(t)^{2}e^{-d_{1}\int_{T}^{t}N(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(T)}\|^{2}+h^{2}(t)\int_{T}^{t}e^{-d_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}N(s)^{-b}ds}\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}N(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Under condition (
3.107
), applying Lemma
A.3
we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{p}_{t}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big[\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}h(t)^{\frac{2(\mu_{6}+1)}{\mu_{6}}}N(t)^{2b}\Big]\Big\}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}

\displaystyle+C\Big\{\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big[\|{\psi_{t}(\tau)}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}h(t)^{2}N(t)^{2b}\Big]\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
3.108
).
∎

Note from (
3.102
) that
\lim_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}=0
provided

\lim_{t\to\infty}\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}=0.

In the following, we can drop the first limit condition.

Corollary 3.19
.

Under the Strict Degree Condition, if
\|\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}
is uniformly bounded on
[0,\infty)
and
\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, then

\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\to 0\quad\text{as }t\to\infty.

(3.113)

Proof.

In this case,
N(t)
and
h(t)
are uniformly bounded on
[2,\infty)
by a constant
C_{0}
. In the Theorem
3.18
, we can replace
N(t)
and
h(t)
by this
C_{0}
. Then conditions in (
3.107
) are met and (
3.113
) follows (
3.108
).
∎

3.4.
Estimates for pressure’s second derivatives

We estimate the Hessian
\nabla^{2}p=\big(\frac{\partial^{2}p}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\big)_{i,j=1,2,\ldots,n}
.
Throughout this subsection
U^{\prime}\Subset U
.

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
U^{\prime}\Subset V\Subset U
and
\delta\in(0,a]
.
Then for
t>0
,

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq C\Big(1+\int_{U}|\nabla p(x,t)|^{(2-\delta)a/\delta}dx\Big)\Big(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}\Big),

(3.114)

where the positive constant
C
depends on
U^{\prime}
,
V
and
\delta
.

Proof.

Young’s inequality gives

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p|^{2-\delta}dx\leq\int_{U^{\prime}}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}dx+\int_{U^{\prime}}K(|\nabla p|)^{-(2-\delta)/\delta}dx\\
\leq\int_{U^{\prime}}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}dx+C\int_{U^{\prime}}\big[1+|\nabla p|^{(2-\delta)a/\delta}\big]dx.

(3.115)

Note that for
s\geq 0
, we have

\frac{1}{2s+2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\nabla\bar{p}|^{2s+2}\zeta^{2}dx=-\int_{U}\bar{p}_{t}\nabla\cdot(|\nabla\bar{p}|^{2s}\nabla\bar{p}\zeta^{2})dx.

(3.116)

Since
\nabla\bar{p}=\nabla p
, replacing
p
by
\bar{p}
in (
3.45
) and using (
3.116
) with
s=0
, we have for any
\zeta(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}(U)
that

\displaystyle\int_{U}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}\zeta^{2}dx\leq C\int_{U}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla p|^{2}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx+C\int_{U}\bar{p}_{t}\nabla\cdot(\nabla p\zeta^{2})dx.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. For the last integral,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{U}\bar{p}_{t}\nabla\cdot(\nabla p\zeta^{2})dx\Big|\leq C\int_{U}|\bar{p}_{t}||\nabla^{2}p|\zeta^{2}+|\bar{p}_{t}||\nabla p|\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{U}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}\zeta^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}|\bar{p}_{t}|^{2}K(|\nabla p|)^{-1}\zeta^{2}dx+C\int_{U}|\bar{p}_{t}||\nabla p|\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)\int_{U}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}\zeta^{2}dx\leq C\int_{U}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla p|^{2}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx

(3.117)

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}|\bar{p}_{t}|^{2}(1+|\nabla p|)^{a}\zeta^{2}dx+C\int_{U}|\bar{p}_{t}||\nabla p|\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx.

Constructing appropriate
\zeta
with
\zeta\equiv 1
on
U^{\prime}
and supp
\zeta\subset V
, we obtain from (
3.117
) that

\displaystyle\int_{U^{\prime}}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}dx\leq C\int_{V}|\nabla p|^{2-a}dx+C\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}\int_{V}(1+|\nabla p|)^{a}dx

\displaystyle+C\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}\int_{V}|\nabla p|dx\leq C\int_{V}|\nabla p|^{2-a}dx+C\Big(1+\int_{V}|\nabla p|dx\Big)(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}).

Thus, by Young’s inequality, we have

\int_{U^{\prime}}K(|\nabla p|)|\nabla^{2}p|^{2}dx\leq C\Big(1+\int_{V}|\nabla p|^{2-a}dx\Big)(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}).

(3.118)

Combining (
3.115
) with (
3.118
), we obtain

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq C\Big(1+\int_{U}|\nabla p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\Big)\Big(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}\Big)\\
+C\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla p(x,t)|^{(2-\delta)a/\delta}dx.

Then (
3.114
) follows.
∎

We consider two cases
\delta=a
and
\delta<a
separately.
We define two exponents

\mu_{9}=\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2+\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{10}=\frac{2-a}{1-a}\big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2\big)+\frac{2}{\mu_{6}+1}.

(3.119)

Theorem 3.21
.

Assume (
SDC
).

(i)
If
t>0
then

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq CL_{7}(1+t^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}})(t+1)^{\mu_{9}}\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}\big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2\big)}\Big\{1+\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\}^{\mu_{10}},

(3.120)

where
L_{7}=(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a})^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2}
and
\mathcal{K}_{1,t}
is defined in (
3.53
).

If
t\geq 3/2
then

\displaystyle\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq CL_{8}\{1+M_{f}(t)\}^{\frac{2}{2-a}\big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2\big)}\cdot\Big\{1+\sup_{[t-3/2,t]}\|{\psi_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\}^{\mu_{10}},

(3.121)

where
L_{8}=(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2}
.

(ii)
If
A<\infty
then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq C\mathcal{A}_{3}^{2\mu_{8}+1},

(3.122)

where
\mathcal{A}_{3}
is defined by (
3.100
).

(iii)
If
\beta<\infty
, then there is
T>0
such that for all
t>T
,

\begin{split}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx&\leq C\Big(1+\beta^{\frac{1}{1-a}}+\sup_{[t-3/2,t]}f^{\frac{2}{2-a}}\Big)^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2}\big(1+\sup_{[t-3/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)^{\mu_{10}}.\end{split}

(3.123)

Above, the positive constant
C
depends on
U^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(i) For
t>0
, we obtain from (
3.114
) with
\delta=a
that

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq C(1+\|{\nabla p}\|^{2-a}_{L^{2-a}})(1+\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}(V)}^{2}).

(3.124)

Then using estimates (
3.88
) and (
3.96
) in (
3.124
), we obtain

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq C\Big\{1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(1+t)\sup_{[0,t]}f+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big\}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2}\\
\quad\cdot\Big\{1+t^{-\frac{1}{2\mu_{6}}}+t^{\frac{1}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}^{2}.

Then (
3.120
) follows.
If
t\geq 3/2
then using (
3.91
) and (
3.97
) in (
3.124
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq C\Big\{1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big\}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+2}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|{\psi_{t}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}^{2}.

Then (
3.121
) follows.

(ii) If
A<\infty
then using (
3.93
) and (
3.103
) in (
3.124
) we obtain

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-a}dx\leq C\mathcal{A}_{2}\mathcal{A}_{3}^{2\mu_{8}}.

This yields (
3.122
).

(iii) If
\beta<\infty
then using (
3.94
) and (
3.104
) in (
3.124
) we obtain (
3.123
).
∎

Next, we treat the case
\delta\in(0,a)
for which we define the following exponents

\displaystyle\nu_{1}=\max\{2,\frac{(2-\delta)a}{\delta}\},

\displaystyle\nu_{2}=2+\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}+\frac{2(\nu_{1}-2)}{2-a},

(3.125)

\displaystyle\nu_{3}=\frac{2-a}{1-a}\Big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1\Big)+\frac{\nu_{1}-a}{1-a},

\displaystyle\nu_{4}=\frac{2}{2-a}\Big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1\Big)+\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2),

(3.126)

\displaystyle\mu_{11}=\frac{2-a}{1-a}\Big(\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1\Big)+\frac{2}{\mu_{6}+1},

\displaystyle\mu_{12}=\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1+\frac{1-a}{2-a}\frac{2}{\mu_{6}+1}.

(3.127)

Theorem 3.22
.

Assume (
SDC
). Let
\delta
be any number in
(0,a)
.

For
t>0
, we have

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq CL_{9}(1+t^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}})(t+1)^{\nu_{2}}\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\nu_{3}}(\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}+1)^{\mu_{11}},

(3.128)

where

L_{9}=(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a})L_{2}(\nu_{1}).

For
t\geq 3/2
, we have

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq CL_{10}(1+M_{f}(t))^{\nu_{4}}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-3/2,t]}\tilde{f}\Big)^{\mu_{12}}\Big\{1+\int_{0}^{t}f(\tau)d\tau\Big\},

(3.129)

where

L_{10}=(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})L_{4}(\nu_{1}).

Above, the positive constant
C
depends on
U^{\prime}
and
\delta
, and
L_{2}(\cdot)
,
L_{4}(\cdot)
are defined in Theorems
3.9
and
3.10
.

Proof.

We use estimate (
3.114
) and utilizing (
3.55
) with
s=\nu_{1}
and (
3.96
) to obtain

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq C\Big\{1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{0}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(1+t)\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,t}\Big\}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1}\\
\quad\cdot\Big\{1+t^{-\frac{1}{2\mu_{6}}}+t^{\frac{1}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\sup_{[0,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}^{2}L_{2}(\nu_{1})(t+1)^{\frac{2(\nu_{1}-2)}{2-a}+1}\mathcal{K}_{1,t}^{\frac{\nu_{1}-a}{1-a}}.

Then (
3.128
) follows. For
t\geq 3/2
, using (
3.60
) for
s=\nu_{1}
instead of (
3.55
) and using (
3.97
) instead of (
3.96
), we obtain

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla^{2}p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq C\Big(1+\|{\bar{p}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+M_{f}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-3/2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau\Big)^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+1}\Big(1+\sup_{[t-1/2,t]}\|\psi_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)^{2}\\
\cdot L_{4}(\nu_{1})\Big(1+M_{f}(t)\Big)^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\Big\{1+\int_{0}^{t}f(\tau)d\tau\Big\}.

Note that
\|\psi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(1+\tilde{f}(t))^{\frac{1-a}{2-a}}
.
Then we obtain (
3.129
).
∎

For asymptotic estimates, we have the following.

Theorem 3.23
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Suppose
\Upsilon_{1}
and
\Upsilon_{2}
defined in Corollary
3.11
are finite numbers.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,a)
, we have

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla p(x,t)|^{2-\delta}dx\leq CL_{4}(\nu_{1})\Upsilon_{1}^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\Upsilon_{2}\mathcal{A}_{3}^{2\mu_{8}},

(3.130)

where
C>0
depends on
U^{\prime}
and
\delta
.

Proof.

Taking limit superior of (
3.114
) as
t\to\infty
and using (
3.65
) for
s=\nu_{1}
, and using (
3.103
), we obtain
(
3.130
).
∎

4.
Dependence on initial and boundary data

In this section, we prove the continuous dependence of solutions
p(x,t)
of the IBVP (
3.1
), (
3.2
) and (
3.3
) with respect to the
L^{\infty}
-norm on the initial data and boundary data. The results are established for either finite time intervals or at time infinity.

Let
p_{1}(x,t)
and
p_{2}(x,t)
be two solutions of the IBVP (
3.7
) having fluxes
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, and initial data
p_{1}(x,0)
and
p_{2}(x,0)
, respectively.
Let
\Psi=\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}
,
P=p_{1}-p_{2}
, and
\bar{P}=P-|U|^{-1}\int_{U}Pdx
. Then by (
3.6
),

\bar{P}(x,t)=\bar{p}_{1}(x,t)-\bar{p}_{2}(x,t)=P(x,t)-\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{U}P(x,0)dx+\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\Psi(x,\tau)d\sigma d\tau.

We will estimate
\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}
where the subset
U^{\prime}
satisfies
U^{\prime}\Subset U
throughout the section.
From (
3.7
) follows

\frac{\partial\bar{P}}{\partial t}=\nabla\cdot\big(K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}\big)-\nabla\cdot\big(K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\big)+\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{\Gamma}\Psi(x,t)d\sigma.

(4.1)

The following quantity will be used throughout this section:

\Lambda(t)=1+\int_{U}\Big(|\nabla p_{1}(x,t)|^{2-a}+|\nabla p_{2}(x,t)|^{2-a}\Big)dx,

4.1.
Results for pressure

First we establish
L^{\infty}
-estimates for
\bar{P}
in terms of its
L^{2}
- and
W^{1,s}
- norms.
We recall that the exponents
\mu_{5}
,
\mu_{6}
and
\mu_{7}
are defined in (
3.69
).

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Let
U^{\prime}\Subset V\Subset U
.
Let
\mu>\mu_{6}^{-1}=\frac{\mu_{5}}{{\mu_{5}}-2}
and denote

{\gamma_{1}}=\mu_{6}-\frac{1}{\mu}=1-\frac{2}{\mu_{5}}-\frac{1}{\mu}\in(0,1).

(4.2)

Then we have for any
T_{0}\geq 0
,
T>0
and
\theta\in(0,1)
that

\sup_{[T_{0}+\theta T,T_{0}+T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\,{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(T_{0},T_{0}+T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(T_{0},T_{0}+T))}\Big),

(4.3)

where the positive constant
C
is independent of
T_{0}
,
T
and
\theta
,

\mathcal{C}_{T_{0},T,\theta}=(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\Big(\lambda\Big[1+\frac{1}{\theta T}\Big]^{1/2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}+\Big(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\|{\Psi}\|\Big)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}},

(4.4)

with

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{T_{0},T}\mathbin{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathbin{=\kern-2.0pt=}}\limits^{\rm def}}}\sup_{t\in[T_{0},T_{0}+T]}\Lambda(t)^{\mu_{7}},

(4.5)

\displaystyle\vartheta=\vartheta_{T_{0},T}\mathbin{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathbin{=\kern-2.0pt=}}\limits^{\rm def}}}\Big[\int_{T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T}\int_{V}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{2\mu(1-a)}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{2\mu(1-a)}\Big)dxdt\Big]^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

(4.6)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
T_{0}=0
.
Let
\zeta(x,t)=\phi(x)\varphi(t)
be a cut-off function with
\varphi(0)=0
and supp
\phi\subset V
.
Same as in Proposition
3.1
we define

\displaystyle\bar{P}^{(k)}=\max\{\bar{P}-k,0\},\quad S_{k}(t)=\{x\in U:\bar{P}^{(k)}(x,t)\geq 0\},

and denote by
\chi_{k}(x,t)
the characteristic function of
S_{k}(t)
.

Multiplying equation (
4.1
) by
\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}
, integrating it over
U
and using integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx=\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta\zeta_{t}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{U}\Big(K(\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\Big)\cdot\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2})dx+\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{\Gamma}\Psi(x,t)d\sigma\int_{U}\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}dx.

Elementary calculations yield

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx\leq\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta|\zeta_{t}|dx-\int_{U}\Big(K(\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\Big)\cdot\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{U}\Big(K(\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\Big)\cdot\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta\nabla\zeta dx+\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{\Gamma}\Psi(x,t)d\sigma\int_{U}\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}dx.

Denoting the last four summands by
I_{i}
,
i=1,2,3,4
, respectively, we rewrite the above as

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx\leq I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

(4.7)

Let
\xi(x,t)=|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|\vee|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|
.
We consider
I_{2}
. Let
J(x,t)=\Big(K(\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\Big)\cdot\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}
.
On the set
U\setminus S_{k}(t)
, since
\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}=0
a.e., we have
J(x,t)=0
a.e..
On the set
S_{k}(t)
, we have
\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}=\nabla\bar{P}
a.e., and, by the monotonicity (
2.19
), we have for almost all
x\in S_{k}(t)
that

\displaystyle J(x,t)

\displaystyle=\big(K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)\nabla\bar{p}_{2}\big)\cdot(\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-\nabla\bar{p}_{2})

\displaystyle\geq(1-a)K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|\vee|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)|\nabla\bar{P}|^{2}=(1-a)K(\xi)|\nabla P^{(k)}|^{2}.

Therefore,

I_{2}\leq-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta^{2}dx.

(4.8)

Note that

|\zeta\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}=|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)-\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}=|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}-2\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)\cdot(\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta)+|\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}.

Thus, (
4.8
) gives

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dx+2(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)||P^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|dx

\displaystyle\quad-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx.

Using Cauchy’s inequality for the second term on the right-hand side of the previous inequality gives

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1-a}{2}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dx+(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1-a}{2}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dx+C\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx.

For the last inequality, we used the fact that
K(\xi)
is bounded above.
For
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we have for any
\varepsilon>0
that

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})^{2}\chi_{k}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx,

(4.10)

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|\Psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{U}\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dx.

(4.11)

Combining (
4.7
), (
4.9
), (
4.1
) and (
4.11
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+\frac{1-a}{2}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2-a}dx\leq C\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|\zeta+|\nabla\zeta|^{2})dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\varepsilon\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|\Psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{U}\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})^{2}\chi_{k}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx.

Integrating in time, taking the supremum in
t
over
[0,T]
, and selecting
\varepsilon=1/(16T)
we find that

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dxdt\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|\zeta+|\nabla\zeta|^{2})dxdt\\
\quad+CT\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dxdt+CT\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})^{2}\chi_{k}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dxdt.

Applying Hölder’s inequality to the last double integral yields

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dxdt

(4.12)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|\zeta+|\nabla\zeta|^{2})dx+CT\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle+CT\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})^{2\mu}|\nabla\zeta|^{2\mu}dxdt\Big)^{1/\mu}\Big(\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big)^{1-1/\mu}.

Under the Strict Degree Condition, by applying Sobolev inequality (
2.30
) with
W(x,t)=K(\xi(x,t))
, we have

\|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(Q_{T})}\leq C\lambda\Big(\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dxdt\Big)^{1/2},

where
\lambda
is defined by (
4.5
). Combining with (
4.12
), we have

\|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(Q_{T})}\leq C\lambda\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|+|\nabla\zeta|^{2})dxdt\Big)^{1/2}\\
+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big(\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big)^{1/2}+T^{1/2}\vartheta\Big(\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}(1-1/\mu)}\Big],

(4.13)

where
\vartheta
is defined by (
4.6
).

Let
M_{0}>0
be fixed which we will determine later.
For integers
i\geq 0
, let
k_{i}=M_{0}(1-2^{-i})
and where
\zeta_{i}
be defined as in (
3.76
) and the sets
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
,
A_{i,j}
be defined similar to (
3.75
) replacing
\bar{p}_{t}
by
\bar{P}
.

Define
F_{i}=\|{\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}\zeta_{i}}\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(A_{i+1,i})}
.
Applying (
4.13
) with
k=k_{i+1}
and
\zeta=\zeta_{i}
we have

\displaystyle F_{i}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Big[\big(2^{i/2}(\theta T)^{-1/2}+2^{i}\big)\|{\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i})}

(4.14)

\displaystyle+2^{i}T^{1/2}\vartheta|A_{i+1,i}|^{\frac{1}{2}(1-1/\mu)}+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2}\Big].

Estimating the same way as in (
3.79
), we have

\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}\zeta_{i}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i+1})}\leq CF_{i}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2-1/{\mu_{5}}}.

(4.15)

Also, similar to (
3.80
), we have

|A_{i+1,i}|\leq C4^{i}M_{0}^{-2}\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{2}.

(4.16)

From estimates (
4.14
), (
4.15
), and the boundedness of
\zeta_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1})}=\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}\zeta_{i}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1})}\leq C\lambda\Big\{2^{i}((\theta T)^{-1/2}+1)\|{\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i})}

\displaystyle\quad+2^{i}T^{1/2}\vartheta|A_{i+1,i}|^{\frac{1}{2}(1-1/\mu)}+T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2}\Big\}|A_{i+1,i}|^{1/2-1/{\mu_{5}}}.

Using (
4.16
) gives

\displaystyle\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda\Big\{2^{i}((\theta T)^{-1/2}+1)\|{\bar{P}^{(k_{i+1})}}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i+1,i})}+4^{i}T^{1/2}\vartheta M_{0}^{-1+1/\mu}\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{1-1/\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{1/2}2^{i}M_{0}^{-1}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}\Big\}2^{i}M_{0}^{-(1-\frac{2}{\mu_{5}})}\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}^{1-\frac{2}{\mu_{5}}}.

Put
Y_{i}=\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}
, the previous inequality gives

\displaystyle Y_{i+1}\leq C\lambda M_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{\mu_{5}}}8^{i}\Big\{\vartheta T^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{0}^{-1+\frac{1}{\mu}}Y_{i}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}+\Big[(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{\frac{1}{2}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}M_{0}^{-1}\Big]Y_{i}\Big\}Y_{i}^{1-\frac{2}{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C8^{i}\Big\{\lambda\vartheta T^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{0}^{-1-{\gamma_{1}}}Y_{i}^{1+{\gamma_{1}}}+\lambda\Big[(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{0}^{-\mu_{6}}\Big]Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{6}}+\lambda T^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}M_{0}^{-1-\mu_{6}}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{6}}\Big\}.

Since
Y_{0}\leq\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}
, aiming at applying Lemma
A.2
with
m=3
we choose
M_{0}
sufficiently large such that

\begin{split}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\leq C\min\Big\{&(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{-1/{\gamma_{1}}}M_{0}^{\frac{1+{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}}},(\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2})^{-1/\mu_{6}}M_{0},\\
&(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{-1/\mu_{6}}M_{0}^{\frac{1+\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\}.\end{split}

Thus, we require

\displaystyle M_{0}

\displaystyle\geq C(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}},\quad M_{0}\geq C(\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2})^{1/\mu_{6}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))},

\displaystyle M_{0}

\displaystyle\geq C(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}.

We choose

\displaystyle M_{0}\geq C\Big((\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+(\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2})^{1/\mu_{6}}+(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big),

(4.17)

for some constant
C>0
.
Since
\lambda\geq 1
and
{\gamma_{1}}<\mu_{6}<1
, we select

\displaystyle M_{0}

\displaystyle=C\Big((\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+(\lambda(1+\frac{1}{\theta T})^{1/2})^{1/\mu_{6}}+(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)

(4.18)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\Big).

Applying Lemma
A.2
and using the same arguments as in Proposition
3.2
, we obtain (
4.3
).
∎

We now derive a variation of Proposition
4.1
. Let
T_{0}=0
.
In the proof of Proposition
4.1
, let
\zeta=\zeta(x)
be the cut-off function such that
\zeta
vanishes in neighborhood of the boundary
\Gamma
.
If
k\geq\|\bar{P}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
, then
\bar{P}^{(k)}(0)=0
and, hence, (
4.13
) holds true with
\zeta_{t}=0
.
In the iteration process, we choose
M_{0}\geq\|\bar{P}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
k_{i}=M_{0}(2-2^{-i})
for
i\geq 0
. This way, we have
k_{i}\geq M_{0}\geq\|\bar{P}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
, thus,
\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}(0)=0
.
Therefore, we can replace
\frac{1}{\theta T}
by
0
in (
4.1
), which becomes

\displaystyle M_{0}

\displaystyle\geq C\Big((\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\lambda^{1/\mu_{6}}+(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big).

Therefore, instead of (
4.18
), we can select

\displaystyle M_{0}

\displaystyle=\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big((\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\lambda^{1/\mu_{6}}+(\lambda T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\Big).

Applying Lemma
A.2
, we have

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}Y_{i}=0

and
\bar{p}(x,t)\leq 2M_{0}
a.e. in
B(x_{0},\rho/4)\times(\theta T,T)
. Proceeding the proof as in Proposition
3.2
, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 4.2
.

Assume the same as in Proposition
4.1
. Then we have for any
T>0
that

\sup_{[0,T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\,\mathcal{C}_{T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\Big),

(4.19)

where

\mathcal{C}_{T}=(\lambda_{T}T^{1/2}\vartheta_{T})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\lambda_{T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}+\Big(\lambda_{T}T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}},

(4.20)

with
\lambda_{T}=\lambda_{0,T}
and
\vartheta_{T}=\vartheta_{0,T}
defined in Theorem
4.1
for
T_{0}=0
.

We will use Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
to obtain specific
L^{\infty}
-estimates of
\bar{P}
in terms of initial and boundary data.
First, we introduce some quantities and parameters.

Same as (
3.24
), we define for
i=1,2
,

f_{i}(t)=\|\psi_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\psi_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{f}_{i}(t)=\|\psi_{it}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\psi_{it}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}.

For
i=1,2
, we assume
f_{i}(t),\tilde{f}_{i}(t)\in C([0,\infty))
and when needed
f_{i}(t)\in C^{1}((0,\infty))
.
Let

A_{i}=\limsup_{t\to\infty}f_{i}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\beta_{i}=\limsup_{t\to\infty}[f_{i}^{\prime}(t)]^{-},

\bar{A}=A_{1}+A_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\beta}=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}.

Let
M_{f_{i}}(t)
,
i=1,2
, be a continuous increasing majorant of
f_{i}(t)
on
[0,\infty)
.
Set

F(t)=f_{1}(t)+f_{2}(t),\quad\ M_{F}(t)=M_{f_{1}}(t)+M_{f_{2}}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{F}(t)=\tilde{f}_{1}(t)+\tilde{f}_{2}(t).

For initial data, set

A_{0}=\|\bar{p}_{1}(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\bar{p}_{2}(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad B_{0}=J_{H}[p_{1}](0)+J_{H}[p_{2}](0).

(4.21)

We recall results from
[
18
]
.

Theorem 4.3

(cf.
[
18
]
, Lemma 5.5 and Theorem 5.6)
.

(i)
For
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Lambda(\tau)^{b}d\tau,

(4.22)

where
b
is defined in (
2.9
).
Consequently, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\cdot M_{1,T}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2},

(4.23)

where
M_{1,T}=A_{0}+T+\int_{0}^{T}\big[f_{1}(\tau)+f_{2}(\tau)\big]d\tau
.

(ii)
Assume the Degree Condition. Then for
t\geq 0

\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq e^{-d_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\Lambda^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-d_{2}\int_{\tau}^{t}\Lambda^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Lambda(\tau)^{b}d\tau.

(4.24)

Moreover, if
\bar{A}<\infty
and

\int_{1}^{\infty}(1+\int_{\tau-1}^{\tau}\tilde{F}(s)ds)^{-b}d\tau=\infty

then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big(1+{\bar{A}}^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau\Big)^{2b}\Big\}.

(4.25)

We now state the continuous dependence in interior
L^{\infty}
-norm.
We use similar quantities to
\mathcal{K}_{1,t}
in (
3.53
) and
\mathcal{K}_{2,t}
(
3.95
), namely,

\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}=1+\sum_{i=1,2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}=1+\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau.

(4.26)

Theorem 4.4
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Let
\mu
be any number satisfying

\mu>\frac{\mu_{5}}{{\mu_{5}}-2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu\geq\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}.

(4.27)

For
T>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}

(4.28)

\displaystyle+CL_{11}M_{2,T}\Big(\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big[\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big),

where
{\gamma_{1}}
is defined by (
4.2
), number
L_{11}>0
depends on the initial data and is defined by (
4.44
) below, number
M_{2,T}>0
depends on the boundary data and is defined by (
4.32
) below.

Proof.

Many exponents will be needed in our proof and are defined here:

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=2(1-a)\mu\geq 2-a,

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}=\frac{\gamma_{2}}{2\mu(1-a)}=1,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}=\frac{1}{2\mu}\Big(\frac{2\gamma_{2}}{2-a}-1\Big)=\frac{2(1-a)}{2-a}-\frac{1}{2\mu},

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}=\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{2\gamma_{4}+1}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)},

(4.30)

\displaystyle\gamma_{6}=\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{\mu_{6}(1-a)}+\frac{\gamma_{3}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}.

(4.31)

We will use notation
\gamma_{3}
in calculations below instead of its explicit value for the sake of generality which will be needed in section
5
.
We prove (
4.28
) with
M_{2,T}
explicitly given by

M_{2,T}=T^{\mu_{20}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}+\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}},

(4.32)

where the exponent
\mu_{20}
is
\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}
in case
T\leq 1
and is
\gamma_{5}+1
in case
T>1
.

We will apply Proposition
4.1
for
T_{0}=0
and Proposition
4.2
.
Fix a subset
V
of
U
such that
U^{\prime}\Subset V\Subset U
.
First, for
M_{1,T}
in (
4.23
) we note that

1+M_{1,T}\leq C\Big\{1+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|{\bar{p}_{i}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(T+1)\Big[(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{i}\|)^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+1\Big]\Big\}\leq C\ell_{0}(T+1)\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}},

(4.33)

where
\ell_{0}=1+A_{0}
.
By (
4.23
) and (
4.33
), we have

\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\leq C\ell_{0}^{1/2}(T+1)^{1/2}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\\
\cdot\Big([T^{1/2}(\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}})]^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+T^{1/2}(\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}})\Big).

(4.34)

Second, we will estimate
{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}
in (
4.4
) with
T_{0}=0
, and
\mathcal{C}_{T}
in (
4.20
).
To simplify our calculations, we will replace
{\mathcal{C}}_{0,T,\theta}
in (
4.4
) by the following upper bound

{\mathcal{C}}_{T,\theta}=\lambda_{T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{(T^{1/2}\vartheta_{T})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+(1+\frac{1}{(\theta T)^{1/2}})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}+(T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\},

(4.35)

and replace
{\mathcal{C}}_{T}
in (
4.20
) by

{\mathcal{C}}_{T}=\lambda_{T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{1+(T^{1/2}\vartheta_{T})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+(T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}.

(4.36)

We now find bounds for involved quantities in (
4.35
) and (
4.36
).
We have from estimate (
3.88
) that

\displaystyle\lambda_{T}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Lambda(t)^{\mu_{7}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big\{1+\|{\bar{p}_{i}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+J_{H}[\bar{p}_{i}](0)+(T+1)\sup_{[0,T]}\|\psi_{i}\|^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{f}_{i}(\tau)d\tau\Big\}^{\mu_{7}}.

Therefore,

\lambda_{T}\leq C\ell_{1}(T+1)^{\mu_{7}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{1-a}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\mu_{7}},

(4.37)

where
\ell_{1}=(1+A_{0}+B_{0})^{2-a}
.
Applying (
3.66
) to
s=\gamma_{2}
and
V
replacing
U^{\prime}
, we have

\vartheta_{T}=\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}\Big[1+|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{\gamma_{2}}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{\gamma_{2}}\Big]dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\leq C\ell_{2}(T+1)^{\gamma_{4}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{3}},

(4.38)

where
\ell_{2}=\big\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}\big(\gamma_{2}+a;[p_{i}(0)]\big)\big\}^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}
.
Also,

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\psi_{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\leq\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}.

(4.39)

We denote

D_{T}=\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

We consider
0<T\leq 1
first. By (
4.34
) and (
4.38
), we respectively have

\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\leq C\ell_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}T^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}D_{T},

(4.40)

\vartheta_{T}\leq C\ell_{2}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{3}}.

(4.41)

By (
4.36
), (
4.37
), (
4.41
) and (
4.39
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{T}

\displaystyle\leq C\ell_{1}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}(T+1)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{\mu_{6}(1-a)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{1+\ell_{2}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{{\gamma_{3}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\ell_{2}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}\leq C\ell_{3}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}},

(4.42)

where
\ell_{3}=\ell_{1}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}\ell_{2}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}.

Note that we used the facts
\gamma_{3}\geq 1
and
{\gamma_{1}}<\mu_{6}<{\gamma_{1}}+1
.
Applying (
4.19
) with the use of (
4.40
) and (
4.42
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\ell_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ell_{3}T^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}+\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\cdot D_{T},

(4.43)

hence, obtaining (
4.28
) for
T\leq 1
with

L_{11}=\ell_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ell_{3}=\ell_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ell_{1}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}\ell_{2}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}.

(4.44)

Consider
T>1
now. By (
4.34
),

\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\leq C\ell_{0}^{1/2}T\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}D_{T}.

(4.45)

Using
\mathcal{C}_{T,\theta}
in (
4.35
) with bounds (
4.37
), (
4.38
) and (
4.39
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{T,\theta}

\displaystyle\leq C\ell_{1}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}(T+1)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{\mu_{6}(1-a)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{1+T^{\frac{1}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\ell_{2}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}(T+1)^{\frac{\gamma_{4}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\gamma_{3}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big\}.

Thus,

{\mathcal{C}}_{T,\theta}\leq C\ell_{3}T^{\gamma_{5}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}.

(4.46)

Therefore, combining (
4.3
), (
4.45
) and (
4.46
) yields

\sup_{[1,T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq CL_{11}T^{\gamma_{5}+1}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}+\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\cdot D_{T}.

(4.47)

Then combining (
4.43
) for
T=1
with (
4.47
), noting that
\gamma_{5}\geq\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}
, we obtain (
4.28
) for
T>1
.
The proof is complete.
∎

Now, we derive asymptotic estimates as
t\to\infty
. Let
t>2
. Applying Proposition
4.1
to
T_{0}=t-1
,
T=1
and
\theta=1/2
, we have

\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(t-1,t))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(t-1,t))}\Big),

(4.48)

where

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)=[\tilde{\lambda}(t)\tilde{\vartheta}(t)]^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+\tilde{\lambda}(t)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}+[\tilde{\lambda}(t)\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\Psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}]^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}

(4.49)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}(t)=\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)^{\mu_{7}},

(4.50)

\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(t)=\Big[\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{V}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{2\mu(1-a)}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{2\mu(1-a)}\Big)dxdt\Big]^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

(4.51)

Before going into specific estimates, we state a general result on the limit superior of
\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}
as
t\to\infty
.
It is of the same spirit as of the
L^{2}
-result (
4.25
).

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume (
SDC
). Suppose

\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)\leq\kappa_{0}g(t),\quad\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)\leq CB(t)

(4.52)

for sufficiently large
t
, with some functions
g(t),B(t)\geq 1
.
Let
\kappa_{1}=d_{2}\kappa_{0}^{-b}
, where
d_{2}
is the positive constant in (
4.24
).
If

\lim_{t\to\infty}B(t)^{\frac{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}{{\gamma_{1}}}}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{2}^{t}g(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}g(t)^{b}\frac{B^{\prime}(t)}{B(t)}=0,

(4.53)

then

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{B(t)\Big(g(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big[g(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big)\Big\}.

(4.54)

Proof.

Let
T>2
be sufficiently large such that (
4.52
) holds for all
t>T
.
By (
4.24
) and (
4.52
), we have for
t^{\prime}>T
that

\displaystyle\|{\bar{P}(t^{\prime})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{t^{\prime}}g^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(T)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{t^{\prime}}g^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\int_{T}^{t^{\prime}}e^{\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{\tau}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau.

Then similar to (
3.112
), we have

\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}(t^{\prime})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\Big(e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{t}g^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(T)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big).

(4.55)

Combining (
4.48
) with (
4.52
) and estimate (
4.55
), we obtain

\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq CB(t)\Big\{e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{t}g^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(T)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big\}^{\varrho_{1}/2}\\
\quad+CB(t)\Big\{e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{T}^{t}g^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(T)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big\}^{1/2},

where
\varrho_{1}=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}
.
Note that
\varrho_{1}<1
, then by condition (
4.53
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{B(t)^{\frac{2}{\varrho_{1}}}\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big\}^{\varrho_{1}/2}

(4.56)

\displaystyle+C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{B(t)^{2}\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\kappa_{1}\int_{\tau}^{t}g^{-b}(\theta)d\theta}\|{\Psi(\tau)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(\tau)^{b}d\tau\Big\}^{1/2}.

The first condition in (
4.53
) and the fact that
B(t)\geq 1
imply
\int_{2}^{\infty}g(\tau)^{-b}d\tau=\infty
.
With this and the second condition in (
4.53
), we apply Lemma
A.3
to each limit in (
4.56
) and obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big(B(t)^{\frac{2}{\varrho_{1}}}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(t)^{2b}\Big)^{\varrho_{1}/2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big(B(t)^{2}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}g(t)^{2b}\Big)^{1/2},

thus, (
4.54
) follows.
∎

Between two required estimates in (
4.52
) of Lemma
4.5
, the second one needs more work. Therefore, we focus on estimating
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)
defined by (
4.49
), which contains
\tilde{\vartheta}(t)
given by (
4.51
). For this one, we have from relation (
2.15
) that

\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C+C\Big(\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{V}K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{\gamma_{2}+a}+K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{\gamma_{2}+a}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq C+C\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{V}K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|)|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{\gamma_{2}+a}+K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{\gamma_{2}+a}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

(4.57)

By (
4.57
) and (
3.59
),

\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(t)\leq CL_{12}B_{1}(t)^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu}}B_{2}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}},

(4.58)

where
L_{12}=\big\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{3}\big(\gamma_{2}+a;[p_{i}(0)]\big)\big\}^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}
,

B_{1}(t)=1+M_{F}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad B_{2}(t)=1+\int_{0}^{t}F(\tau)d\tau.

Theorem 4.6
.

Assume (
SDC
). Suppose
\bar{A}<\infty
.
Define

\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}=1+\sup_{[0,\infty)}(\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}),\quad\omega(t)=1+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau,\quad B_{3}(t)=\omega(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}B_{2}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}.

Let
d_{4}=d_{2}d_{3}^{-b}
, where
d_{2}
is in (
4.24
) and
d_{3}
is in (
4.61
) below.
If

\lim_{t\to\infty}B_{3}(t)^{\frac{2(\gamma_{1}+1)}{\gamma_{1}}}e^{-d_{4}\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{-\frac{2b}{1-a}}\int_{2}^{t}\omega(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}(\omega^{b}(t))^{\prime}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\omega^{b}(t)F(t)}{\int_{0}^{t}F(\tau)d\tau}=0,

(4.59)

then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Upsilon_{4}\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{B_{3}(t)\Big(\omega(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big[\omega(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\gamma_{1}+1}}\Big)\Big\},

(4.60)

where
\Upsilon_{4}
is defined by (
4.64
) below.

Proof.

Since
\bar{A}<\infty
, we have
\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}<\infty
.
Then

\bar{A}\leq C(\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{2}+\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}})\leq C\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}<\infty.

By (
3.92
), there is
T_{1}>0
such that for
\tau>T_{1}
, we have

\Lambda(\tau)\leq C\Big(\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}+\int_{\tau-1}^{\tau}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau\Big),

hence, for
t>T_{1}+1
,

\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)\leq C\Big(\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau\Big)\leq d_{3}\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}\omega(t),\quad\tilde{\lambda}(t)\leq C\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{1-a}}\omega(t)^{\mu_{7}},

(4.61)

where
d_{3}
is a positive constant.
Note also that
\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}
for all
t\geq 0
.
Then by (
4.49
), (
4.61
) and (
4.58
), we have

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)\leq C\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{(1-a)\mu_{6}}}\omega(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big(1+L_{12}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}B_{2}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}+\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\Big)\\

(4.62)

Thus,

\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)\leq C\eta_{1}\omega(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}B_{2}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}=C\eta_{1}B_{3}(t),

(4.63)

where
\eta_{1}=L_{12}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{(1-a)\mu_{6}}}\big[\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}+\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\big]
.
Using (
4.61
) and (
4.63
), we apply Lemma
4.5
with
g(t)=\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}\omega(t)

and
B(t)=\eta_{1}B_{3}(t)
. Note that the last two limits in (
4.59
) imply the second limit in (
4.53
).
As a result, we obtain from (
4.54
) that

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{\eta_{1}B_{3}(t)\Big[\Big([\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}\omega(t)]^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)^{\varrho_{1}}+[\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}}\omega(t)]^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big]\Big\}.

Hence (
4.60
) follows with

\Upsilon_{4}=\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2b}{1-a}}\eta_{1}=L_{12}^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{(1-a)\mu_{6}}+\frac{2b}{1-a}}\big[\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}+\bar{\Upsilon}_{3}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}+1}}\big].

(4.64)

The proof is complete.
∎

Next, we will treat the case
\bar{A}=\infty
. For that we recall some estimates from
[
18
]
.

Lemma 4.7

(cf.
[
18
]
, Lemma 5.8)
.

Assume the Degree Condition and
\bar{A}=\infty
. Define

\displaystyle W_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=1+M_{F}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau,

\displaystyle W_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=1+\bar{\beta}^{\frac{1}{1-a}}+F(t-1)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+F(t)+\int_{t-1}^{t}F(\tau)+\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau.

(i)
There is
T_{1}>0
such that
\Lambda(t)\leq CW_{1}(t)
for all
t>T_{1}
.

(ii)
If
\bar{\beta}<\infty
then there is
T_{2}>0
such that
\Lambda(t)\leq W_{2}(t)
for all
t>T_{2}
.

Let
W(t)
be defined, in the general case, by

W(t)=1+M_{F}(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau,

and, in case
\bar{\beta}<\infty
, by

W(t)=1+\bar{\beta}^{\frac{1}{1-a}}+\sup_{[t-2,t]}F(t)^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau.

Then for large
t
, we have from Lemma
4.7
that

\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)\leq d_{5}W(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\lambda}(t)\leq CW(t)^{\mu_{7}},

(4.65)

where
d_{5}
is a positive constant.
With (
4.65
), we restate Lemma
4.5
as the following.

Lemma 4.8
.

Assume (
SDC
). Suppose
\bar{A}=\infty
and
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)\leq C\tilde{B}(t)
for sufficient large
t
, with some function
\tilde{B}(t)\geq 1
.
Let
d_{6}=d_{2}d_{5}^{-b}
, where
d_{2}
is in (
4.24
) and
d_{5}
is in (
4.65
).
If

\lim_{t\to\infty}\tilde{B}(t)^{\frac{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}{{\gamma_{1}}}}e^{-d_{6}\int_{2}^{t}W(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}W(t)^{b}\frac{\tilde{B}^{\prime}(t)}{\tilde{B}(t)}=0,

(4.66)

then

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{\tilde{B}(t)\Big(W(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big[W(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big)\Big\}.

(4.67)

Denote

\mu_{13}=\max\Big\{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}},\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}+1}+\frac{1-a}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}\Big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{7}=\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu}.

(4.68)

Theorem 4.9
.

Assume (
SDC
). Suppose
\bar{A}=\infty
and
\int_{0}^{\infty}F(\tau)d\tau=\infty
.
Define

B_{4}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}F(\tau)d\tau\quad\text{and}\quad B_{5}(t)=M_{F}(t)^{\gamma_{7}}B_{4}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

Let
d_{6}
be defined as in Lemma
4.8
.
If

\lim_{t\to\infty}W(t)^{\frac{2\mu_{13}({\gamma_{1}}+1)}{{\gamma_{1}}}}B_{5}(t)^{\frac{2}{{\gamma_{1}}}}e^{-d_{6}\int_{2}^{t}W^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}=0,

(4.69)

\lim_{t\to\infty}(W^{b}(t))^{\prime}=0,\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}W^{b}(t)\frac{M_{F}^{\prime}(t)}{M_{F}(t)}=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}W^{b}(t)\frac{F(t)}{\int_{0}^{t}F(\tau)d\tau}=0,

(4.70)

then

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

(4.71)

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big\{W(t)^{\mu_{13}}B_{5}(t)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big(W(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big[W(t)^{b}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big]^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big)\Big\}.

Proof.

Let

B_{6}(t)=1+\sup_{[0,t]}(\|\psi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\psi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}).

Then
\lim_{t\to\infty}B_{6}(t)=\infty
and
\lim_{t\to\infty}B_{4}(t)=\infty
.
Note that
B_{6}(t)\leq(1+M_{F}(t))^{\frac{1-a}{2-a}}
, and in both cases of definition of
W(t)
, we have

\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq CW(t)^{\frac{1-a}{2-a}\frac{2-a}{2}}=CW(t)^{\frac{1-a}{2}}.

(4.72)

We estimate
\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)
. By (
4.57
) and (
3.61
), we have for
t
sufficiently large that

\displaystyle\tilde{\vartheta}(t)

\displaystyle\leq CM_{F}(t)^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)}{2\mu}}B_{4}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}=CB_{5}(t).

(4.73)

By (
4.49
), (
4.65
), (
4.73
) and (
4.72
),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{W(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}+B_{5}(t)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}W(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}+W(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}+1}}W(t)^{\frac{1-a}{2(\mu_{6}+1)}}\Big\}\leq CB_{7}(t),

where
B_{7}(t)=W(t)^{\mu_{13}}B_{5}(t)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}
.
We apply Lemma
4.8
with
\tilde{B}(t)=B_{7}(t)
.
The first condition in (
4.66
) is replaced by

\lim_{t\to\infty}(W(t)^{\mu_{13}}B_{5}(t)^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}})^{\frac{2}{\varrho_{1}}}e^{-d_{6}\int_{2}^{t}W^{-b}(\tau)d\tau}=0,

which is (
4.69
). The second condition in (
4.66
) is replaced by (
4.70
).
Then we obtain (
4.71
) directly from (
4.67
).
∎

Example 4.10
.

Suppose that for
t
sufficiently large, we have

F(t)\leq M_{F}(t)\leq Ct^{\theta_{1}},\quad W(t)\leq Ct^{\theta_{2}/b},

for some
\theta_{1}>0
and
0<\theta_{2}<1
.
Following the proof of Theorem
4.9
, we can see that the statements still hold true if the functions
F(t)
,
M_{F}(t),W(t)
are replaced by their upper bounds
Ct^{\theta_{1}}
,
Ct^{\theta_{1}}
,
Ct^{\theta_{2}/b}
, respectively.
With such replacements, conditions (
4.69
) and (
4.70
) are met, and
B_{5}(t)=Ct^{\theta_{3}}
, where
\theta_{3}=\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)\theta_{1}+\theta_{1}+1}{2\mu}
.
Therefore, from (
4.71
), it follows

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\Big(t^{\theta_{5}}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}+[t^{\theta_{4}}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}]^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big),

where

\theta_{4}=\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)\theta_{1}+\theta_{1}+1}{2\mu{\gamma_{1}}}+\frac{\theta_{2}\mu_{13}({\gamma_{1}}+1)}{b{\gamma_{1}}}+\theta_{2}

and

\theta_{5}=\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}+a-2)\theta_{1}+\theta_{1}+1}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}+\frac{\theta_{2}\mu_{13}}{b}+\theta_{2}
.

4.2.
Results for pressure gradient

In
[
18
]
, the norm
\|{\nabla P(t)}\|_{L^{2-a}}
is estimated for all
t>0
. Now we estimate

\|{\nabla P(t)}\|_{L^{s}(U^{\prime})}
and
\|\nabla P\|_{L^{s}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}
for any
s\in(2-a,2)
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
\delta\in(0,a)
.
Then for all
t>0
,

\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{1+\sum_{i=1,2}\Big[\|\bar{p}_{it}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla p_{i}(t)\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+\|\psi_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big]\Big\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
\cdot\Big(1+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}},

(4.74)

and for any
T>0
,

\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq C\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{1/2}\\
\cdot\Big[T+\sum_{i=1,2}\Big(\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}|^{2-a}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\psi_{i}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
\cdot\Big[T+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla p_{i}|^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\Big]^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(4.75)

Here, constant
C>0
depends on
U
,
U^{\prime}
and
\delta
.

Proof.

Note that
\nabla\bar{P}=\nabla P
.
Let
\zeta=\zeta(x)
be a cut-off function such that
\zeta
vanishes in neighborhood of
\Gamma
.
Multiplying equation (
4.1
) by
\bar{P}\zeta^{2}
and integrating over
U
, using integration by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{U}\bar{P}_{t}\bar{P}\zeta^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{U}\Big(K(\nabla p_{1}|)\nabla p_{1}-K(|\nabla p_{2}|)\nabla p_{2}\Big)\cdot(\nabla P\zeta^{2}+2\bar{P}\zeta\nabla\zeta)dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{\Gamma}\Psi(x,t)d\sigma\int_{U}\bar{P}\zeta^{2}dx.

Let
\xi(x,t)=|\nabla p_{1}|\vee|\nabla p_{2}|
. By the monotonicity (
2.19
) in Lemma
2.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{U}\bar{P}_{t}\bar{P}\zeta^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla P\zeta|^{2}dx+2\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{1-a}|P|\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx

(4.76)

\displaystyle+\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{U}|\bar{P}|\zeta^{2}dx.

Let
V\Subset U
such that
U^{\prime}\Subset V
. We select
\zeta
such that
\zeta\equiv 1
on
U^{\prime}
and supp
\zeta\subset V
.
We obtain from (
4.76
) that

\displaystyle(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla P\zeta|^{2}dx\leq\int_{U_{1}}|\bar{P}_{t}||\bar{P}|dx+C\int_{V}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{1-a}|\bar{P}|dx+C\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}})\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+C\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}|^{2-2a}dx\Big)^{1/2}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+C\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\psi_{i}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Hence

\int_{U^{\prime}}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dx\leq C\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}}+\Big(\int_{U}(1+\sum_{i=1,2}|\nabla p_{i}|)^{2-a}dx\Big)^{1/2}+\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\psi_{i}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big].

(4.77)

By Hölder’s inequality and property (
2.13
), we have

\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla P|^{2-\delta}dx\leq C\Big(\int_{U^{\prime}}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dx\Big)^{\frac{2-\delta}{2}}\Big(\int_{U^{\prime}}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}.

(4.78)

Combining (
4.78
) with (
4.77
) yields

\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\{1+\sum_{i=1,2}\Big[\|\bar{p}_{it}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{2-a}dx+\|\psi_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big]\Big\}^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
\cdot\Big(\int_{U^{\prime}}(1+|\nabla p_{1}(x,t)|+|\nabla p_{2}(x,t)|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}},

then we obtain (
4.74
).
We now prove (
4.75
).
Integrating (
4.77
) in
t
over
[0,T]
and applying Hölder’s inequality yield

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dx\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\Big)^{1/2}\cdot\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{p}_{1t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\bar{p}_{2t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{2-2a}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)^{1/2}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dx\leq C\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}

(4.79)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}\Big(\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(1+|\nabla p_{i}|^{2-a})dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\psi_{i}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Again, by Hölder’s inequality and property (
2.13
), we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla P|^{2-\delta}dxdt\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{2-\delta}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}.

(4.80)

Using (
4.79
) in (
4.80
) and taking the power
1/(2-\delta)
, we obtain (
4.75
).
∎

We now have explicit estimates in terms of initial and boundary data.

Theorem 4.12
.

Assume (
SDC
).
For
\delta\in(0,a)
,
0<t_{0}<1
and
T>t_{0}
we have

\sup_{[t_{0},T]}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq CM_{3,t_{0},T}(\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.81)

where
M_{3,t_{0},T}
is defined in (
4.85
) below.

Proof.

We use estimate (
4.74
). We bound
\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{2}}
by (
4.23
):

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\cdot M_{1,T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big),

(4.82)

where
M_{1,T}=A_{0}+T+\int_{0}^{T}\big[f_{1}(\tau)+f_{2}(\tau)\big]d\tau
.
We estimate time derivative by (
3.90
), estimate
\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}|^{2-a}dx
by (
3.88
), then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,2}\Big[\|\bar{p}_{it}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{2-a}dx+\|\psi_{i}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big]

(4.83)

\displaystyle\leq C\,t_{0}^{-1}\Big(\sum_{i=1,2}L_{5}(t_{0};[p_{i}(0),\psi_{i}])\Big)+(T+1)\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}\Big).

Recalling
\nu_{1}=\max\{2,\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}\}
, we apply (
3.55
) with
s=\nu_{1}
and obtain

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\leq\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{U^{\prime}}1+|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{\nu_{1}}dx\\
\leq C\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{2}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])\Big](T+1)^{\frac{2(\nu_{1}-2)}{2-a}+1}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}-a}{1-a}}.

(4.84)

Combining (
4.82
), (
4.83
) and (
4.84
) with (
4.74
), we have (
4.81
) with

M_{3,t_{0},T}=(1+t_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4}})M_{1,T}^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{5}(t_{0};[p_{i}(0),\psi_{i}])\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{2}\big(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)]\big)\Big]^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}(T+1)^{\gamma_{8}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{9}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{1}{4}},

(4.85)

where

\gamma_{8}=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}\big(\frac{2(\nu_{1}-2)}{2-a}+1\big)
and

\gamma_{9}=\frac{2-a}{4(1-a)}+\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}\big(\frac{\nu_{1}-a}{1-a}\big).

The proof is complete.
∎

As
t\to\infty
, we have the following asymptotic estimate.

Theorem 4.13
.

Assume (
SDC
). Let

\bar{\Upsilon}_{1}=1+\sup_{[0,\infty)}F,\ \bar{\Upsilon}_{2}=1+\int_{0}^{\infty}F(t)dt\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=1+\bar{A}^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{F}(\tau)d\tau.

If
\bar{\Upsilon}_{1}
,
\bar{\Upsilon}_{2}
and
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
are finite then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Upsilon_{5}\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2},

(4.86)

where
\Upsilon_{5}
is defined by (
4.87
) below.

Proof.

Taking limsup of (
4.74
) and making use the limits (
4.25
), (
3.93
) and estimate (
3.65
) give

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1/2}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{\frac{b}{2}}\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{4}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])\bar{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\bar{\Upsilon}_{2}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
4.86
) with

\Upsilon_{5}=\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{2}^{\frac{b}{2}+\frac{1}{4}}\Big\{\bar{\Upsilon}_{1}^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\bar{\Upsilon}_{2}\sum_{i=1,2}L_{4}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(4.87)

∎

Remark 4.14
.

Even though the limit estimate in (
4.86
) still depends on the initial data presented in
\Upsilon_{5}
, the smallness of the estimate can be controlled by the difference
\Psi(t)
for large
t
.

The estimate in Theorem
4.12
blows up when
t_{0}\to 0
.
To overcome this, we consider the Lebesgue norm in both
x
and
t
.

Theorem 4.15
.

Assume (
SDC
). Let
\delta\in(0,a)
. For any
T>0
, we have

\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq CM_{4,T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}},

(4.88)

where
M_{4,T}
is defined in (
4.92
) below.

Proof.

Applying (
3.66
) to
s=\nu_{1}\geq 2
we have

T+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\leq T+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\nu_{1}}dxdt\\
\leq C\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\nu_{1}+a,[p_{i}(0)])\Big](T+1)^{\frac{2\nu_{1}}{2-a}-1}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}}{1-a}}.

(4.89)

Inequality (
3.88
) provides

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}\leq C(1+A_{0}+B_{0})(T+1)\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}.

Using (
3.28
) we see that

T+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}|^{2-a}dxdt\leq C(1+A_{0})(T+1)\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}.

Hence,

T+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Big\{\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{2-2a}dxdt+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\psi_{i}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big\}\leq CN_{1,T},

(4.90)

where
N_{1,T}=(1+A_{0}+B_{0})(T+1)\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}.

According to (
4.22
) and (
3.88
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq CT\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Lambda(t)^{b}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)

(4.91)

\displaystyle\leq CTN_{1,T}^{b}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big).

Combining (
4.75
), (
4.89
), (
4.90
) and (
4.91
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq CT^{\frac{1}{4}}N_{1,T}^{\frac{b}{4}}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\Psi(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}dt\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot N_{1,T}^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big\{\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\nu_{1}+a,[p_{i}(0)])\Big](T+1)^{\frac{2\nu_{1}}{2-a}-1}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}}{1-a}}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
4.88
) with

M_{4,T}=(1+A_{0}+B_{0})^{\frac{b+1}{4}}\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\nu_{1}+a,[p_{i}(0)])\Big]^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}T^{\frac{1}{4}}(T+1)^{\gamma_{10}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{11}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{2,T}^{\frac{b+1}{4}},

(4.92)

where

\gamma_{10}=\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}\Big(\frac{2\nu_{1}}{2-a}-1\Big)+\frac{b+1}{4}
and

\gamma_{11}=\frac{\nu_{1}}{1-a}\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}+\frac{(b+1)(2-a)}{4(1-a)}
.
∎

5.
Dependence on the Forchheimer polynomial

In this section we study the dependence of solutions of IBVP (
3.7
) on the coefficients of the Forchheimer polynomial
g(s)
in (
2.2
).
Let
N\geq 1
, the exponent vector
\vec{\alpha}=(0,\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{N})
and the boundary data
\psi(x,t)
be fixed.

Let
{\bf D}
be a compact subset of
\{\vec{a}=(a_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{N}):a_{0},a_{N}>0,a_{1},\ldots,a_{N-1}\geq 0\}
.
Set

\hat{\chi}({\bf D})=\max\{\chi(\vec{a}):\vec{a}\in{\bf D}\}.

Then
\hat{\chi}({\bf D})
is a number in
[1,\infty)
.

Let
g_{1}(s)=g(s,\vec{a}^{(1)})
and
g_{2}(s)=g(s,\vec{a}^{(2)})
be two functions of class FP(
N,\vec{\alpha}
), where
\vec{a}^{(1)}
and
\vec{a}^{(2)}
belong to
{\bf D}
. For
k=1,2
, let
p_{k}=p_{k}(x,t;\vec{a}^{(k)})
be the solution of (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) with
K=K(\xi,\vec{a})
and the same boundary flux
\psi
.

Let
P=p_{1}-p_{2}
and
\bar{P}=P-|U|^{-1}\int_{U}Pdx
.
