Then

\bar{P}_{t}=\nabla\cdot(K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|,\vec{a}^{(1)})\nabla\bar{p}_{1})-\nabla\cdot(K(|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|,\vec{a}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{p}_{2})\quad\text{in }U\times(0,\infty)

(5.1)

As shown in
[
17
]
, all constants
d_{j}
,
c_{j}
,
C_{j}
and
C
appearing in estimates in the previous sections when
\vec{a}
varies among the vectors
\vec{a}^{(1)}
,
\vec{a}^{(2)}
,
\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee\vec{a}^{(2)}
and
\vec{a}^{(1)}\wedge\vec{a}^{(2)}
, can be made dependent of
\hat{\chi}({\bf D})
, but independent of
\vec{a}
.
We still denote them by
d_{j}
,
c_{j}
,
C_{j}
and
C
, respectively, in this section.

Throughout this section, we continue to have
U^{\prime}\Subset U
.
The flux-related quantities
f(t)
,
\tilde{f}(t)
,
M_{f}(t)

A
and
\beta
are defined in section
3
, from (
3.24
)
to (
3.25
).
Also,
\mathcal{K}_{1,t}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2,t}
are defined in (
3.53
) and (
3.95
), respectively.
Regarding initial data,
A_{0}
is defined in (
4.21
).

5.1.
Results for pressure

We will estimate the interior
L^{\infty}
-norm for
\bar{P}(x,t)
in terms of
\bar{P}(x,0)
and
|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|
.
We start with the following general estimates which are counter parts of Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
.

Proposition 5.1
.

We use the same assumptions and notation as in Proposition
4.1
.

(i)
There exists a positive constant
C=C(U,V,U^{\prime})
such that

\sup_{[T_{0}+\theta T,T_{0}+T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(T_{0},T_{0}+T))}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(T_{0},T_{0}+T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big),

(5.2)

where

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}=[\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-\frac{1}{2}})]^{\frac{1}{\mu}_{6}}+[\lambda(T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1}+\vartheta_{2})]^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}},

(5.3)

with
\lambda=\lambda_{T_{0},T}
defined in (
4.5
), and

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1}

\displaystyle=\vartheta_{1,T_{0},T}\mathbin{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathbin{=\kern-2.0pt=}}\limits^{\rm def}}}\Big(\int_{T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T}\int_{V}|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{2(1-a)\mu}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{2(1-a)\mu}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}},

(5.4)

\displaystyle\vartheta_{2}

\displaystyle=\vartheta_{2,T_{0},T}\mathbin{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mathbin{=\kern-2.0pt=}}\limits^{\rm def}}}\Big(\int_{T_{0}}^{T_{0}+T}\int_{V}|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{(2-a)\mu}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{(2-a)\mu}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

(5.5)

(ii)
There exists a positive constant
C=C(U,V,U^{\prime})
such that

\sup_{[0,T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big),

(5.6)

where

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T}=\lambda_{T}^{\frac{1}{\mu}_{6}}+[\lambda_{T}(T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1,T}+\vartheta_{2,T})]^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}},

(5.7)

with
\lambda_{T}=\lambda_{0,T}
,
\vartheta_{1,T}=\vartheta_{1,0,T}
and
\vartheta_{2,T}=\vartheta_{2,0,T}
.

Proof.

(i) Let
\zeta(x,t)
be a cut-off function such that it is piecewise linear continuous in
t
,
\zeta(\cdot,0)=0
and for each
t
, supp
\zeta(\cdot,t)\Subset V
.
Let
\bar{P}^{(k)}=\max\{\bar{P}-k,0\}
and
\chi_{k}
be characteristic function of the set
\{(x,t):\bar{P}^{(k)}>0\}
.

Let
\xi=|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|\vee|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|
.
Multiplying equation (
5.1
) by
\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}
and integrating over
U
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx-\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta\zeta_{t}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{U}(K(|\nabla p_{1}|,\vec{a}^{(1)})\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla p_{2}|,\vec{a}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{p}_{2})\cdot\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{U}(K(|\nabla p_{1}|,\vec{a}^{(1)})\nabla\bar{p}_{1}-K(|\nabla p_{2}|,\vec{a}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{p}_{2})\cdot[2P^{(k)}\zeta\nabla\zeta]dx=I_{1}+I_{2}.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. For
I_{2}
, we use (
2.13
) to estimate

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})|\bar{P}^{(k)}|\zeta|\nabla\zeta|dx

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\int_{S_{k}}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{2(1-a)}\chi_{k}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx.

For
I_{1}
, similar to estimate (
4.8
) of term
I_{2}
in Proposition
4.1
but using monotonicity (
2.18
) in place of (
2.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{1-a})|\nabla\bar{P}^{(k)}|\zeta^{2}dx.

Similar to the way (
4.9
) was derived from (
4.8
), one obtains for
\varepsilon>0
that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1-a}{2}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dx+C\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{2-a})\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dx.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+\frac{1-a}{2}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta|\zeta_{t}|dx+C\int_{U}K(\xi)|P^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx+\varepsilon\int_{S_{k}}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+C\varepsilon^{-1}\int_{U}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{2(1-a)}\chi_{k}|\nabla\zeta|^{2}dx+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{2-a})\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dx.

Let
J=\sup_{[0,T]}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta|^{2}dx+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla(\bar{P}^{(k)}\zeta)|^{2}dxdt
.
Integrating the preceding inequality in time, taking supremum in
t
over
[0,T]
, selecting
\varepsilon=1/(8T)
and applying Hölder’s inequality yield

\displaystyle J\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}\zeta|\zeta_{t}|dxdt+C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|P^{(k)}\nabla\zeta|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle\quad+CT\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{2(1-a)\mu}|\nabla\zeta|^{2\mu}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\mu}}\Big\{\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}

\displaystyle\quad+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{S_{k}}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{(2-a)\mu}\zeta^{2}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\mu}}\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}\chi_{k}\zeta^{2}dxdt\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}.

Note that
\vec{a}^{(i)}
and
\vec{a}^{(i)}
belong to the compact set
{\bf D}
, hence
|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\leq C
. Then

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|+|\nabla\zeta|)dxdt

\displaystyle\quad+CT\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{2(1-a)\mu}|\nabla\zeta|^{2\mu}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\mu}}\Big\{\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big\{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{S_{k}}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{(2-a)\mu}\zeta^{2}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{\mu}}\Big\{\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big\}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}.

Using inequality (
2.30
) in Lemma
2.4
with
W(x,t)=K(\xi(x,t))
, we have

\displaystyle\|{P^{(k)}\zeta}\|_{L^{\mu_{5}}(Q_{T})}\leq C\lambda J\leq C\lambda\Big\{\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\bar{P}^{(k)}|^{2}(|\zeta_{t}|+|\nabla\zeta|)dxdt\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+T^{1/2}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}\Big(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|\Big)^{2(1-a)\mu}|\nabla\zeta|^{2\mu}dxdt\Big]^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Big\{\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\mu}}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}(|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|)^{(2-a)\mu}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Big\{\iint_{Q_{T}\cap{\rm supp}\zeta}\chi_{k}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

Let
Y_{i}=\|\bar{P}^{(k_{i})}\|_{L^{2}(A_{i})}
. In the same way as in proof of Proposition
4.1
we find that

\displaystyle Y_{i+1}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda M_{0}^{-1+\frac{2}{\mu_{5}}}2^{i}\Big\{(1+(\theta T)^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}+T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1}M_{0}^{-1+\frac{1}{\mu}}Y_{i}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}+\vartheta_{2}M_{0}^{-1+\frac{1}{\mu}}Y_{i}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}\Big\}Y_{i}^{1-\frac{2}{\mu_{5}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda 2^{i}\Big\{M_{0}^{-\mu_{6}}(1+(\theta T)^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{6}}+T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1}M_{0}^{-1-{\gamma_{1}}}Y_{i}^{1+{\gamma_{1}}}+\vartheta_{2}M_{0}^{-1-{\gamma_{1}}}Y_{i}^{1+{\gamma_{1}}}\Big\},

where the exponents
{\gamma_{1}},\mu_{6}
are defined in Theorem
4.1
.
Since
Y_{0}\leq\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}
we choose
M_{0}
sufficiently large such that

\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}\leq C\min\Big\{((\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}})^{-\frac{1}{\mu}_{6}}M_{0},(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1})^{-\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}}}M_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}+1},M_{0}^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}+1}(\lambda\vartheta_{2})^{-\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}}}\Big\},

thus

\displaystyle M_{0}\geq C\max\Big\{

\displaystyle(\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{\mu}_{6}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))},(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}},

\displaystyle(\lambda\vartheta_{2})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big\}.

Using the fact that

(\lambda(1+(\theta T)^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{\mu}_{6}}
,
(\lambda T^{1/2}\vartheta_{1})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}
,
(\lambda\vartheta_{2})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}
are less than or equal to
C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}
,
we then choose

M_{0}=C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T_{0},T,\theta}\Big(\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}+\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big).

Then applying Lemma
A.2
for
m=3
to sequence
\{Y_{i}\}
, and using the same argument as in Theorem
4.1
, we obtain
|\bar{p}(x,t)|\leq M_{0}
in
U^{\prime}\times(\theta T,T)
.

(ii) Using same arguments as for deriving Proposition
4.2
from the proof of Proposition
4.1
, we can modify the proof in part (i) to obtain (
5.6
).
∎

We recall some results from
[
18
]
which will be needed in subsequent developments.

Theorem 5.2

(cf.
[
18
]
, Theorems 5.16 and 5.17)
.

(i)
For
0<T<\infty
, we have

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+CM_{5,T}|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|,

(5.8)

where
M_{5,T}=A_{0}+T+\int_{0}^{T}f(\tau)d\tau
.

(ii)
Assume the Degree Condition.
Suppose
\sup_{[0,\infty)}f(t)<\infty
and
\sup_{[1,\infty)}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau<\infty
.
Then

\sup_{[1,\infty)}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\Upsilon_{6}^{b+1}|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|,

(5.9)

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\mathcal{A}_{2}^{b+1}|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|,

(5.10)

where
\Upsilon_{6}=1+A_{0}+\sup_{[0,\infty)}f^{\frac{2}{2-a}}(t)+\sup_{[1,\infty)}\int_{t-1}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau
,
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is defined by (
3.99
).

We will take advantage of calculations in Theorem
4.4
. However the exponents will be changed.
The new counterparts of exponents
\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\ldots,\gamma_{6}
in (
4.29
)–(
4.31
) are

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=(2-a)\mu,

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}^{\prime}=\frac{\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}{2\mu(1-a)}=\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}\geq 1,

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2\mu}\Big(\frac{2\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}{2-a}-1\Big)=1-\frac{1}{2\mu},

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}^{\prime}=\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{2\gamma_{4}^{\prime}+1}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)},

\displaystyle\gamma_{6}^{\prime}=\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{\mu_{6}(1-a)}+\frac{\gamma_{3}^{\prime}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}.

Firstly, we replace
\gamma_{2}
by
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}
in estimate (
4.38
) to obtain

\vartheta_{2,T}\leq C(T+1)^{\gamma_{4}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{3}^{\prime}}=C\ell_{2}^{\prime}(T+1)^{\gamma_{4}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{3}^{\prime}},

(5.11)

where
\ell_{2}^{\prime}=[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a;[p_{i}(0)])]^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}
.
Secondly, since
\gamma_{4}^{\prime}\geq 1/(2\mu)
, it follows

\vartheta_{1,T}\leq CT^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}+\vartheta_{2,T}\leq C\ell_{2}^{\prime}(T+1)^{\gamma_{4}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{3}^{\prime}}.

(5.12)

Thirdly, same as (
4.37
), we have

\lambda_{T}\leq C\ell_{1}^{\prime}(T+1)^{\mu_{7}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}(2-a)}{1-a}}\mathcal{K}_{2,T}^{\mu_{7}},

(5.13)

where
\ell_{1}^{\prime}=1+A_{0}+\|{\nabla p_{1}(0)}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+\|{\nabla p_{2}(0)}\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}
.

For finite time intervals, we have the following estimates.

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume (
SDC
).

(i)
For
T\in(0,1]
, we have

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+CM_{6,T}\big(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{A}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\big),

(5.14)

where
M_{6,T}
is defined in (
5.17
) below and

\mathcal{A}=\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/2}
.

(ii)
For
T>1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[1,T]}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq CM_{7,T}\Big(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{A}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big),

(5.15)

where
M_{7,T}
is defined in (
5.18
) below.

Proof.

(i)
Let
T\in(0,1]
.
Applying (
5.6
) of Proposition
5.1
gives

\displaystyle\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\Big\{T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+(T^{1/2}\sup_{[0,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}})^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T}T^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\Big\{1+\sup_{[0,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}\Big\}.

Using (
5.11
), (
5.12
) and (
5.13
) to estimate corresponding terms in (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\leq C\lambda_{T}^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}(\vartheta_{1,T}+\vartheta_{2,T})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}=C\ell_{3}^{\prime}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}},

where
\ell_{3}^{\prime}=(\ell_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}(\ell_{2}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}
.
Moreover, by (
5.8
)

\sup_{[0,T]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{1}{2}})\Big[1+A_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}f(\tau)d\tau\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\mathcal{A}(1+A_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}.

(5.16)

Combining the above, we obtain (
5.14
) with

M_{6,T}=\ell_{3}^{\prime}(1+A_{0})^{1/2}T^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}^{\prime}+\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\mathcal{K}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}.

(5.17)

(ii) Let
T>1
. We apply (
5.2
) with
T_{0}=0
and
\theta T=1
. Note that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{0,T,\theta}\leq C\lambda_{T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}(1+[T^{1/2}(\vartheta_{1,T}+\vartheta_{2,T})]^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}})\leq C\ell_{3}^{\prime}T^{\gamma_{5}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}.

Then similar calculations to those for proving (
4.47
) in Theorem
4.4
lead to
(
5.15
) with

M_{7,T}=(1+A_{0})^{1/2}\ell_{3}^{\prime}T^{\gamma_{5}^{\prime}+1}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\gamma_{6}^{\prime}+\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}\mathcal{K}_{2,T}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}.

(5.18)

The proof is complete.
∎

We now consider estimates when
t\to\infty
. We use the same notation as in subsection
4.1
.
For
t\geq 2
, we apply Proposition
5.1
(i) to the interval
[t-1,t]
, that is
T_{0}=t-1
and
T=1
, and use
\theta=1/2
. Then we have

\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t)\Big\{\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big\},

(5.19)

where

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t)=\tilde{\lambda}(t)^{\frac{1}{\mu_{6}}}(1+\widehat{\vartheta}(t))^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}},

(5.20)

with
\tilde{\lambda}(t)
defined by (
4.50
), and

\widehat{\vartheta}(t)=1+\Big(\int_{t-1}^{t}\int_{V}|\nabla\bar{p}_{1}|^{(2-a)\mu}+|\nabla\bar{p}_{2}|^{(2-a)\mu}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}.

(5.21)

Note that
\widehat{\vartheta}(t)
is the same as
\tilde{\vartheta}(t)
in (
4.51
) with exponent
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=(2-a)\mu
replacing
\gamma_{2}=2(1-a)\mu
.

By (5.53) in
[
18
]
, there is
d_{7}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{U}\bar{P}^{2}dx\leq-d_{7}\Lambda^{-b}(t)\int_{U}\bar{P}^{2}dx+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\Lambda(t).

(5.22)

Hence, for
t^{\prime}\geq 1

\displaystyle\|{\bar{P}(t^{\prime})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-d_{7}\int_{1}^{t^{\prime}}\Lambda(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{1}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-d_{7}\int_{\tau}^{t^{\prime}}\Lambda(\theta)^{-b}d\theta}\Lambda(\tau)d\tau.

(5.23)

Similar to (
4.55
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}(t^{\prime})}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-d_{7}\int_{0}^{t}\Lambda(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{1}^{t}e^{-d_{7}\int_{\tau}^{t}\Lambda(\theta)^{-b}d\theta}\Lambda(\tau)d\tau.

(5.24)

Let
\Upsilon_{2}
be defined as in Corollary
3.11
, and

\Upsilon_{3}=1+\sup_{[0,\infty)}\|{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad\Upsilon_{7}=1+\sup_{[2,\infty)}\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau.

We have the following result for unbounded time intervals.

Theorem 5.4
.

Assume (
SDC
). Suppose
\Upsilon_{2}
,
\Upsilon_{3}
and
\Upsilon_{7}
are finite numbers.
Then we have

\sup_{[2,\infty)}\|\bar{P}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Upsilon_{8}(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{A}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}),

(5.25)

where
\Upsilon_{8}
is defined by (
5.30
) below, and
\mathcal{A}
is defined in Theorem
5.3
.

Proof.

Use (
4.61
) to bound
\Lambda(t)
and
\tilde{\lambda}(t)
, we have for all
t\geq 1
that

\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)\leq C\Upsilon_{7}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}},\quad\tilde{\lambda}(t)\leq C\Upsilon_{7}^{\mu_{7}}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{1-a}}.

(5.26)

Moreover, using (
4.58
) to bound
\widehat{\vartheta}(t)
with
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}
replacing
\gamma_{2}
, we obtain

\widehat{\vartheta}(t)\leq CL^{\prime}_{12}B_{1}(t)^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a-2)}{2\mu}}B_{2}(t)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\leq CL^{\prime}_{12}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a-2)}{2\mu}}\Upsilon_{2}^{\frac{1}{2\mu}},

(5.27)

where
L^{\prime}_{12}=\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{3}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a,[p_{i}(0)])\}^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}
.
It follows from (
5.20
), (
5.26
) and (
5.27
) that

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t)\leq C\eta_{2},\quad\text{where }\eta_{2}=\Upsilon_{7}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Upsilon_{2}^{\frac{1}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}(L^{\prime}_{12})^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{2\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}(1-a)}+\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a-2)}{2\mu({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}.

(5.28)

Then we have from (
5.19
) and (
5.28
) that

\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\eta_{2}\Big\{\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big\}.

(5.29)

By (
5.24
) and (
5.26
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|{\bar{P}(1)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|(\Upsilon_{7}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}})^{b+1}.

We estimate
\|{\bar{P}(1)}\|_{L^{2}}
by (
5.16
) with
T=1
and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\eta_{3}\mathcal{A},\quad\text{where }\eta_{3}=(1+A_{0})^{1/2}\mathcal{K}_{1,1}^{\frac{2-a}{2(1-a)}}+(\Upsilon_{7}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{2}{1-a}})^{\frac{b+1}{2}},

with
\mathcal{K}_{1,1}
defined by (
3.53
).
Combining this with (
5.29
), we obtain (
5.25
) with

\Upsilon_{8}=\eta_{2}\eta_{3}.

(5.30)

The proof is complete.
∎

For results as
t\to\infty
, we have:

Theorem 5.5
.

Assume the same as in Theorem
5.4
. Then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Upsilon_{9}\Big(|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big)^{1/2},

(5.31)

where
\Upsilon_{9}
is defined by (
5.35
) below.

Proof.

Define
B_{7}(t)=1+A^{\frac{2}{2-a}}+\int_{t-2}^{t}\tilde{f}(\tau)d\tau
.
Note that
\limsup_{t\to\infty}B_{7}(t)\leq 2\mathcal{A}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is defined by (
3.99
).
By (
3.92
), for
t
sufficiently large we have

\sup_{\tau\in[t-1,t]}\Lambda(\tau)\leq CB_{7}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\lambda}(t)\leq CB_{7}(t)^{\mu_{7}}.

(5.32)

Using (
5.32
) and (
5.27
) to estimate
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}(t)
in (
5.20
), we derive from (
5.19
) for large
t
that

\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\eta_{4}B_{7}(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}\Big\},

(5.33)

where

\eta_{4}=\big[L^{\prime}_{12}\Upsilon_{2}^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Upsilon_{3}^{\frac{\mu_{4}(\gamma_{2}^{\prime}+a-2)}{2\mu}}\big]^{\frac{1}{{\gamma_{1}}+1}}.

Similar to (
4.55
) and (
5.24
), for large
T
and
t>T
, we have

\sup_{[t-1,t]}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq Ce^{-C\int_{T}^{t}B_{7}(\tau)^{-b}d\tau}\|{\bar{P}(T)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{T}^{t}e^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}B_{7}(\theta)^{-b}d\theta}B_{7}(\tau)d\tau.

(5.34)

Since
B_{7}(t)
is bounded, one can easily see from (
5.33
) and (
5.34
) that

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta_{4}\Big\{\limsup_{t\to\infty}B_{7}(t)^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\}\Big\{|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{T}^{t}e^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}B_{7}(\theta)^{-b}d\theta}B_{7}(\tau)d\tau\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\Big[\limsup_{t\to\infty}\int_{T}^{t}e^{-C\int_{\tau}^{t}B_{7}(\theta)^{-b}d\theta}B_{7}(\tau)d\tau\Big]^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\Big\}.

Applying Lemma
A.3
with
h\equiv 1
to the last two limits, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta_{4}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}}\Big\{|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{b+1}{2}}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{(b+1){\gamma_{1}}}{2({\gamma_{1}}+1)}}\Big\}.

Then (
5.31
) follows with

\Upsilon_{9}=\eta_{4}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{\mu_{7}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{b+1}{2}}.

(5.35)

The proof is complete.
∎

Similar to Remark
4.14
, estimate (
5.31
) shows that the smallness of
\|\bar{P}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(U^{\prime})}
as
t\to\infty
can be controlled by
|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|
.

5.2.
Results for pressure gradient

We now study the dependence for pressure gradient.
The results are parallel to those in subsection
4.2
.
For
i=1,2
, we denote
H_{i}(\xi)=H(\xi,a^{(i)})
defined in (
2.16
), and recall that the functional
J_{H}[\cdot]
is defined by (
3.26
).

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
\delta\in(0,a)
and
U^{\prime}\Subset V\Subset U
. There exists a constant
C>0
depending on
U
,
U^{\prime}
,
V
and
\delta
such that for any
t>0
, we have

\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1+\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1,2}J_{H_{i}}[p_{i}](t)\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\cdot\Big(1+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{V}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}},

(5.36)

and for any
T>0
, we have

\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq C\Big\{\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(T+\sum_{i=1,2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{0}^{T}J_{H_{i}}[p_{i}](t)dt\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\\
+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(T+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{0}^{T}J_{H_{i}}[p_{i}](t)dt\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\}\cdot\Big\{T+\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(5.37)

Proof.

Let
\zeta(x,t)
be the same cut-off function as in Proposition
4.11
.
Multiplying equation (
5.1
) by
\bar{P}\zeta^{2}
, integrating over
U
and using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{U}\bar{P}_{t}\bar{P}\zeta^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{U}\Big(K(\nabla p_{1}|,\vec{a}^{(1)})\nabla p_{1}-K(|\nabla p_{2}|,\vec{a}^{(2)})\nabla p_{2}\Big)\cdot(\nabla P\zeta^{2}+2\bar{P}\zeta\nabla\zeta)dx.

Again, the fact
\nabla\bar{P}=\nabla P
was used in the above.
Let
\xi(x)=|\nabla p_{1}|\vee|\nabla p_{2}|
. By the monotonicity (
2.18
),

\displaystyle\int_{U}\bar{P}_{t}\bar{P}\zeta^{2}dx\leq-(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi,\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee a^{(2)})|\nabla P\zeta|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\quad+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|^{1-a}+|\nabla p_{2}|^{1-a})|\nabla P|\zeta^{2}dx+C\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{1-a}|\bar{P}|\zeta dx.

Hence,

\displaystyle(1-a)\int_{U}K(\xi,\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee a^{(2)})|\nabla P\zeta|^{2}dx\leq C\|{\bar{P}_{t}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big(\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{2-2a}dx\Big)^{1/2}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla p_{2}|^{2-a})dx.

By constructing appropriate function
\zeta(x)\in[0,1]
with
\zeta\equiv 1
on
V
, we have

\int_{V}K(\xi,\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee a^{(2)})|\nabla P|^{2}dx\leq\int_{U}K(\xi,\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee a^{(2)})|\nabla P|^{2}\zeta^{2}dx\\
\leq C\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}\Big\{\|{\bar{p}_{1t}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\bar{p}_{2t}}\|_{L^{2}}+\Big(\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{2-2a}dx\Big)^{1/2}\Big\}\\
+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla p_{2}|^{2-a})dx.

(5.38)

Using (
4.78
) with
K(\xi)=K(\xi,\vec{a}^{(1)}\vee a^{(2)})
and (
5.38
) we obtain

\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\|{\bar{P}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}\Big\{\Big(1+\|{\bar{p}_{1t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\bar{p}_{2t}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+J_{H_{1}}[p_{1}]+J_{H_{2}}[p_{2}]\Big)^{1/4}\\
\quad+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/2}\Big(1+J_{H_{1}}[p_{1}]+J_{H_{2}}[p_{2}]\Big)^{1/2}\Big\}\Big(\int_{V}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dx\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

Then simply due to
|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\leq C
, inequality (
5.36
) follows.

To prove (
5.37
), we have from (
5.38
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}K(\xi)|\nabla P|^{2}dxdt

(5.39)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{p}_{1t}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\bar{p}_{2t}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{2-2a}dxdt\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+C|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla p_{2}|^{2-a})dxdt.

According to (
4.80
),

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla P|^{2-\delta}dxdt\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}K(\xi)|\nabla P\zeta|^{2}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{2-\delta}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}.

(5.40)

Using (
5.38
) in (
5.40
), we obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U^{\prime}}|\nabla P|^{2-\delta}dxdt\\
\leq C\Big\{\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|{\bar{p}_{1t}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|{\bar{p}_{2t}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{2-2a}dxdt\Big)^{1/2}\\
+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}(|\nabla p_{1}|^{2-a}+|\nabla p_{2}|^{2-a})dxdt\Big\}^{\frac{2-\delta}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{V}(1+|\nabla p_{1}|+|\nabla p_{2}|)^{\frac{a(2-\delta)}{\delta}}dxdt\Big)^{\frac{\delta}{2}}.

Thus, we have (
5.37
).
∎

Theorem 5.7
.

Assume (
SDC
). For
\delta\in(0,a)
,
0<t_{0}<1
, and
T>t_{0}
we have

\sup_{[t_{0},T]}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq CM_{8,t_{0},T}(\|\bar{P}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.41)

where
M_{8,t_{0},T}
is defined in (
5.42
) below.

Proof.

Using estimates (
5.8
), (
3.90
), (
3.88
) and (
4.84
) in (
5.36
), we obtain (
5.41
) with

M_{8,t_{0},T}=M_{5,T}^{1/4}\Big(t_{0}^{-1}\sum_{i=1,2}L_{5}(t_{0};[p_{i}(0)])+(T+1)\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,T}\Big)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\Big\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{2}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])(T+1)^{\frac{2(\nu_{1}-2)}{2-a}+1}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}-a}{1-a}}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(5.42)

∎

Theorem 5.8
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Suppose
\Upsilon_{1}
,
\Upsilon_{2}
(defined in (
3.63
)) and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
(defined by (
3.99
)) are finite.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,a)
, we have

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime})}\leq C\Upsilon_{10}|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/4},

(5.43)

where
\Upsilon_{10}
is defined by (
5.44
) below.

Proof.

Taking limsup of (
5.36
) and making use the limits (
5.10
), (
3.93
) and estimate (
3.65
) with
s=\nu_{1}
yield

\limsup_{t\to\infty}\|\nabla P(t)\|_{L^{2-\delta}}\leq C\Big(\mathcal{A}_{2}^{b+1}|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\Big)^{1/4}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{1/2}\Big\{\sum_{i=1,2}L_{4}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])\Upsilon_{1}^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\Upsilon_{2}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
5.43
) with

\Upsilon_{10}=\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\frac{b+3}{4}}\Big\{\Upsilon_{1}^{\mu_{4}(\nu_{1}-2)}\Upsilon_{2}\sum_{i=1,2}L_{4}(\nu_{1};[p_{i}(0)])\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(5.44)

∎

Theorem 5.9
.

Assume (
SDC
).
Let
\delta\in(0,a)
and
T>0
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
U,U^{\prime},\delta
such that

\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq CM_{9,T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{1/2}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/2}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/4}\Big),

(5.45)

where
M_{9,T}
is defined by (
5.48
) below.

Proof.

Define
N_{2,T}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|{\bar{p}_{i}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\nabla p_{i}(0)\|_{L^{2-a}}^{2-a}+(T+1)\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{2-a}{1-a}}+\mathcal{K}_{2,T}
.
From (
3.28
) and (
3.88
) we have

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|{\bar{p}_{it}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{U}|\nabla p_{i}(x,t)|^{2-a}dxdt\leq CN_{2,T}.

(5.46)

Neglecting the negative term on the right-hand side of (
5.22
) and integrating in
t
twice yield

\|{\bar{P}}\|_{L^{2}(U\times(0,T))}^{2}\leq CT\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\int_{0}^{T}\Lambda(t)dt\Big)\leq CTN_{2,T}\Big(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|\Big).

(5.47)

Combining (
5.46
), (
5.47
), (
4.89
) and (
5.37
) we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla P\|_{L^{2-\delta}(U^{\prime}\times(0,T))}\leq C\Big\{T^{1/4}N_{2,T}^{1/4}(\|{\bar{P}(0)}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|)^{1/4}\cdot N_{2,T}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\quad+|\vec{a}^{(1)}-\vec{a}^{(2)}|^{1/2}N_{2,T}^{1/2}\Big\}\cdot\Big\{\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\nu_{1}+a;[p_{i}(0)])\Big](T+1)^{\frac{2\nu_{1}}{2-a}-1}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}}{1-a}}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
5.45
) with

M_{9,T}=(T+1)^{\frac{1}{4}}N_{2,T}^{1/2}\Big\{\Big[\sum_{i=1,2}L_{1}(\nu_{1}+a;[p_{i}(0)])\Big](T+1)^{\frac{2\nu_{1}}{2-a}-1}\mathcal{K}_{1,T}^{\frac{\nu_{1}}{1-a}}\Big\}^{\frac{\delta}{2(2-\delta)}}.

(5.48)

The proof is complete.
∎

Appendix A
Auxiliaries

We recall a classical result on fast decaying geometry sequences.

Lemma A.1

(cf.
[
20
]
, Chapter II, Lemma 5.6)
.

Let
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
be a sequence of non-negative numbers satisfying

Y_{i+1}\leq AB^{i}Y_{i}^{1+\mu},\quad i=0,1,2,\cdots,

(A.1)

where
A>0
,
B>1
and
\mu>0
.
Then

Y_{i}\leq A^{\frac{(1+\mu)^{i}-1}{\mu}}B^{\frac{(1+\mu)^{i}-1}{\mu^{2}}-\frac{i}{\mu}}Y_{0}^{(1+\mu)^{i}},\quad i=0,1,2,\cdots.

(A.2)

Consequently, if
Y_{0}\leq A^{-1/\mu}B^{-1/\mu^{2}}
then
\displaystyle{\lim_{i\to\infty}Y_{i}=0}
.

The following generalization is an important ingredient for our iteration technique used in this paper.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
be a sequence of non-negative numbers satisfying

Y_{i+1}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}A_{k}B_{k}^{i}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{k}},\quad i=0,1,2,\cdots,

(A.3)

where
A_{k}>0
,
B_{k}>1
and
\mu_{k}>0
for
k=1,2,\ldots,m
.
Let
B=\max\{B_{k}:1\leq k\leq m\}
and
\mu=\min\{\mu_{k}:1\leq k\leq m\}
. Then the following statement holds true.

\text{If}\quad\sum_{k=1}^{m}A_{k}Y_{0}^{\mu_{k}}\leq B^{-1/\mu}\quad\text{then }\lim_{i\to\infty}Y_{i}=0.

(A.4)

In particular,

\displaystyle\text{if}\quad Y_{0}\leq\min\{(m^{-1}A_{k}^{-1}B^{-\frac{1}{\mu}})^{1/\mu_{k}}:1\leq k\leq m\}\quad\text{then }\lim_{i\to\infty}Y_{i}=0.

(A.5)

Proof.

It is obvious that (
A.5
) is a consequence of (
A.4
) when we require

A_{k}Y_{0}^{\mu_{k}}\leq m^{-1}B^{-1/\mu}
for each
k=1,2,\ldots,m
.
We now prove (
A.4
). To make a clear presentation, we consider
m=2
. The proof can be robustly modified to cover general
m
.
Therefore, we consider a sequence
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
of non-negative numbers that satisfies

Y_{i+1}\leq B^{i}(A_{1}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{1}}+A_{2}Y_{i}^{1+\mu_{2}})\quad\text{for all }i\geq 0,

where
A_{1},A_{2}>0
,
B>1
and
0<\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
, and
Y_{0}
satisfies

A_{1}Y_{0}^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}Y_{0}^{\mu_{2}}\leq B^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}}.

(A.6)

Note, in this case, that
\mu=\mu_{1}
.

Claim.
If there is
D>0
such that

Y_{0}\leq D(A_{1}D^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{\mu_{2}})^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}}B^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}^{2}}}\leq D,

(A.7)

then

Y_{i}\leq D\quad\text{for all }i\geq 0,

(A.8)

and

\lim_{i\to\infty}Y_{i}=0.

(A.9)

Proof of the Claim.

First, we prove (
A.8
) by induction.
By condition (
A.7
), the inequality (
A.8
) holds for
i=0
.
Let
i\geq 0
. Assume

Y_{j}\leq D
for all
0\leq j\leq i
.
We then have for
0\leq j\leq i
that

\displaystyle Y_{j+1}

\displaystyle\leq B^{j}(A_{1}Y_{j}^{1+\mu_{1}}+A_{2}Y_{j}^{1+\mu_{2}})=B^{j}\Big[A_{1}D^{1+\mu_{1}}\Big(\frac{Y_{j}}{D}\Big)^{1+\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{1+\mu_{2}}\Big(\frac{Y_{j}}{D}\Big)^{1+\mu_{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq B^{j}(A_{1}D^{1+\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{1+\mu_{2}})\Big(\frac{Y_{j}}{D}\Big)^{1+\mu_{1}}\leq B^{j}\Big[\frac{A_{1}D^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{\mu_{2}}}{D^{\mu_{1}}}\Big]Y_{j}^{1+\mu_{1}}.

Then

Y_{j+1}\leq\tilde{A}B^{j}Y_{j}^{1+\mu_{1}}
for all
0\leq j\leq i
, where
\tilde{A}=(A_{1}D^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{\mu_{1}})/D^{\mu_{1}}
.
Note from (
A.7
) that

Y_{0}\leq\tilde{A}^{-1/\mu_{1}}B^{-1/\mu_{1}^{2}}\leq D.

(A.10)

Applying Lemma
A.1
to sequence
\{Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{i+1},0,0,\ldots\}
, we have

\displaystyle Y_{i+1}\leq\tilde{A}^{\frac{(1+\mu_{1})^{i+1}-1}{\mu_{1}}}B^{\frac{(1+\mu_{1})^{i+1}-1}{\mu_{1}^{2}}-\frac{i+1}{\mu_{1}}}Y_{0}^{(1+\mu_{1})^{i+1}}.

This and the first inequality in (
A.10
) give

Y_{i+1}\leq\tilde{A}^{-1/\mu_{1}}B^{-1/\mu_{1}^{2}}B^{-\frac{i+1}{\mu_{1}}}.

(A.11)

It follows from this and the second inequality in (
A.10
) that

Y_{i+1}\leq\tilde{A}^{-1/\mu_{1}}B^{-1/\mu_{1}^{2}}\leq D
.
Thus we obtain (
A.8
) for
i+1
, and by the induction principle, statement (
A.8
) must hold true for all
i\geq 0
.
Since (
A.8
) now holds, the estimate (
A.11
) also holds for all
i
, thus the limit (
A.9
) follows.
The proof of Claim is complete.

By the virtue of the Claim, it suffices to find
D>0
that satisfies two inequalities in (
A.7
).
Let
D>0
be the unique positive number such that
A_{1}D^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{\mu_{1}}=B^{-1/\mu_{1}}
.
Obviously,
D(A_{1}D^{\mu_{1}}+A_{2}D^{\mu_{2}})^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}}B^{-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}^{2}}}=D
.
Then the second inequality in (
A.7
) is satisfied.
Since (
A.6
) implies
D\geq Y_{0}
, the first inequality in (
A.7
) is also satisfied. Our proof is complete.
∎

The following is a useful inequality in estimating limits superior of solutions.

Lemma A.3
.

For
f(t)\geq 0
,
g(t),h(t)>0
,
y(t)\geq 0
,
t>T
, let

y(t)=h(t)\int_{T}^{t}e^{-\int_{\tau}^{t}g(\theta)d\theta}f(\tau)d\tau.

Assume

\int_{T}^{\infty}g(t)dt=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h^{\prime}(t)}{h(t)g(t)}=0.

(A.12)

Then

\limsup_{t\to\infty}y(t)\leq\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)f(t)}{g(t)}.

(A.13)

Proof.

Let
\delta>0
. We have for
t>T
that

\displaystyle y^{\prime}=\Big(-g+\frac{h^{\prime}}{h}\Big)y+hf=-\Big(1-\frac{h^{\prime}}{hg}\Big)gy+hf.

By the second condition in (
A.12
), for sufficiently large
t
, we have

\displaystyle y^{\prime}\leq-(1-\delta)gy+hf.

With the first condition in (
A.12
), we apply Lemma A.1(ii) in
[
19
]
and obtain

\limsup_{t\to\infty}y(t)\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\limsup_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)f(t)}{g(t)}.

Letting
\delta\to 0
, we obtain (
A.13
).
∎
